  - 56th Infantry Brigade, 20 August 1944: a representative point (Caen sector), confidence 0.3; the attachment to the 59th Division and the XII Corps relation closed on 19 August (old value no end, new value 19 August), because the brigade was integral to the 49th Division from 20 August (Ellis).
  - 115th Infantry Brigade, 23 March 1945: a representative point (Xanten) on the XII Corps Rhine front, confidence 0.3; only the order of battle places it.
  - 1st Special Service Brigade, about 20 January 1945 (Blackcock): the day is approximate (no source read gives it), confidence 0.3; the 1944 relations to I Corps and the 6th Airborne Division closed on 8 September 1944 (old value no end, new value 8 September), when the brigade returned to England (Wikipedia).
  - 4th Special Service Brigade, end of November 1944: the date is taken as 30 November (Stacey says "the end of November"), confidence 0.3; the 3rd Canadian Division attachment of D-Day closed on 15 September 1944 (old value no end, new value 15 September), the day before the Dunkirk row.
  - Corps relations of the brigades start on the row dates where the sources do not date them.
- **Corrections to the planning list (infantry and commandos):** the 56th Infantry Brigade was formed on 15 February 1944 and became part of the 49th Division on 20 August 1944 (Ellis, Appendix IV; Wikipedia), so only 6 June to 20 August 1944 is in scope; the 115th Infantry Brigade arrived in North-West Europe on 12 February 1945, not in April (Wikipedia), and only Ellis's Rhine order of battle places it (TracesOfWar puts "the 115th" at Flushing, which was the 155th Brigade of the 52nd Division); the 1st Special Service Brigade returned to England on 8-9 September 1944 and came back for Blackcock in January 1945, renamed the 1st Commando Brigade.
- **2nd Canadian Armoured Brigade (7 claims, C1707 to C1713).** Le Mesnil-Patry (11 June 1944), Colombelles in Atlantic (18 July), Saint-André-sur-Orne under the 2nd Canadian Division (21 July), the Totalize start line (7 August), Calais (25 September), short of Breda under the Polish Armoured Division (27 October) and the Calcar ridge in Blockbuster (26 February 1945); one new place. The brigade's existing D-Day row (C0001) is unchanged. The II Canadian Corps relation starts at 3 p.m. on 11 July 1944, as Stacey states (p. 166). Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new, unless stated): the brigade's relation to Second Army from D-Day (CR0002) and the attachment to the 3rd Canadian Division (CR0001) are closed the day before the next row under another higher unit (old value no end, new value 10 June and 20 July 1944); the other corps and division relations start on the row dates where the sources do not date them; the 26 February 1945 attachment to the 2nd Canadian Division stands for two regiments of the brigade.
- **Corrections to the planning list (Canadian):** the Moyland woods action of 19 February 1945 (Ellis, vol. II, p. 268) gives only that regiments of the brigade were to relieve the Guards tank battalions, with no place for the brigade, so the planned row was replaced by the Calcar ridge row of 26 February (Stacey, p. 492).
- **Semantic scenario 23 (12 expectations, 356 in all).** It checks the 56th Infantry Brigade's moves of 11-12 June 1944 and the end of its independent period, the 27th Armoured Brigade with no relation after its break-up, the 1st Special Service Brigade with no relation while in England, the 4th Special Service Brigade at Walcheren, the 2nd Canadian Armoured Brigade under II Canadian Corps and at Breda, the 4th Armoured Brigade at Verden, and the batch's first and last rows. Writing it showed that the 27th Armoured Brigade's two relations still ran on after the break-up: the I Corps relation (CR0977) now ends on 29 July 1944, stated by the break-up row C1676 (old value no end, new value 29 July 1944, reason the break-up, Ellis vol. I p. 522), and the 3rd Division attachment (CR0978) is closed on the same day as an inferred value (end between 18 and 29 July).

## cleanup-2-late-war-west: follow-up after Scott's answers (29 October 2026)

Scott accepted the five recommendations of the audit (`docs/review/cleanup2_audit.md`). One commit per item.

- **Row-by-row read of the 84 rows with two cited families (open question 1): 6 raised from 0.5 to 0.7, 78 unchanged.** A row was raised only where both source families give the day and the place. In each raised row the place is an area, an objective town or an unsurveyed landmark, so the rubric value is 0.7 (approximate place, two families), not 0.9. Old value 0.5, new value 0.7, with the reason in a Review sentence of each record:
  - 82nd Airborne Division, landing at Groesbeek, 17 September 1944 (TracesOfWar and the division's Operation Market historical data).
  - 82nd Airborne Division, command post CHAMPION west of Groesbeek, 17 September 1944 (same two sources).
  - 4th Armored Division, landing at Utah Beach, 11 July 1944 (USHMM and Wikipedia).
  - VI Corps, Dragoon assault, 15 August 1944 (CMH Riviera to the Rhine and Wikipedia).
  - VIII Corps, Avranches entered, 30 July 1944 (CMH Breakout and Pursuit and Wikipedia).
  - 89th Infantry Division, Rhine crossing at St. Goar, 26 March 1945 (CMH Last Offensive and Wikipedia).
  The other 78 were left: in them the second family gives only the unit, the army, the operation dates, a month, or a span, or the place rests on one family, or the date or place is inferred or representative.
- **Four British front-line rows widened to 10 km (open question 2).** Old precision, new precision 10 km; the point Ellis names, the place, the date and the confidence are unchanged; the reason is that each row is a corps or army front, and the sector rule is 10 km. Each record has a Review sentence. Rows: Second Army, 29 August 1944 (Vernon, 5 km); XII Corps, 4 August 1944 (Évrecy, 5 km); XXX Corps, 21 August 1944 (the Touques near Gacé, 3 km) and 17 September 1944 (Valkenswaard, 3 km). Validator warnings fall from 24 to 20.
- **Open questions 3 to 5 accepted with no change:** the 7th Armored Division command post of 13 August 1944 stays as the CMH list gives it; the 231 date gaps are left for a later batch; the 9 open conflict groups wait for a new source. Recorded in the audit report.

## cleanup-2-late-war-west: clean-up of the late-war Western Front data (29 October 2026)

Scott asked for a review and clean-up pass of the Western Front data (6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945, division level and above, all nationalities) using only the sources already in the data. The audit is `docs/review/cleanup2_audit.md`. One commit per item.

- **Duplicate places merged (4).** Four places were entered twice by different batches: Hébécrevon (Manche), Stolberg (Rhineland), Évrecy (Calvados) and Bains-les-Bains (Vosges). In each pair the older row survives; the newer row is retired in `data/place_merges.csv` (the id is not reused) and its name is kept as an alias of the survivor. Seven claims were re-pointed: the old value is the retired place, the new value is the survivor, the reason is that both rows are the same commune; each claim note says so. Coordinates did not change: for Hébécrevon, Stolberg and Bains-les-Bains the two points were identical; the two Évrecy claims of the British batch keep their own Nominatim point (about 100 m from the Wikipedia point of the survivor) with a `coord_override` that says so.
- **Operation date warning cleared (1 claim).** The 7th Armoured Division row of 8 August 1944 (Mont Pinçon, making for Condé-sur-Noireau) is labelled Operation Bluecoat, whose end date (7 August) comes from the Wikipedia infobox, while Ellis describes the Bluecoat fighting to 8 August. No value changed: the row is listed in `data/operation_exceptions.csv` with that reason, which removes one validator warning (25 to 24).
- **Confidence raised (1 claim).** The First Army (German) row of 8 September 1944, its transfer to Army Group G: old 0.5, new 0.7. Reason: the rubric gives 0.7 to an approximate place with two source families; the CMH (Lorraine Campaign p. 193) and the OKW war diary (Schramm p. 370) both give the day and both describe the army sector that the representative point (Metz, 50 km) stands for. The audit found 227 rows at 0.3 or 0.5 that cite references of two families. The notes of the 76 that state two families were read: in every other one the second family covers only the day, only the unit or only the context, gives a month-level date, or the point is a representative headquarters point (0.3 by the rubric), so none was raised. 67 pair the CMH with the World War II by Day compilation of the same CMH command-post lists, which is not a second family, so they stay. The other 84 were not read one by one; they are an open question in the audit.
- **Resolved open questions removed from the documents (Scott asked for it; documents only, no data changed).** Each was checked against a merged change, Scott's answer or the changelog before it was removed; open questions that are still unresolved were kept, and the remaining items keep their numbers. Removed:
  - `docs/roadmap.md`: the 2d French Armored Division's attachment to XV Corps (written by the French batch); XXII and XXIII Corps as placeholders (activated); the 95th Division's April corps and the 100th Division's April parent (both answered by Scott on 27 October 2026); the AT and CZ country codes (approved by Scott); the 243rd Division's destroyed flag (settled by the clean-up of 28 October 2026, in three lists); the Panzer Lehr and 716th Division conflict groups (settled on 28 October 2026; the other three groups stay open); the 353rd Division's corps (settled by note on 28 October 2026); the end of the 7th Armoured and Guards Armoured attachments to II Canadian Corps (closed by British Batch 1, two lists); extending I Corps in the British batch (done); an operation row for Tractable (added) and for Plunder (added); the 2nd Canadian Division's Boxtel reserve row (written by Canadian Batch 2); the Polish, Belgian, Dutch and Czechoslovak formations and the 11th Armoured Division's attachment to the Canadian corps (Allied contingents batch and British Batch 2, two lists); the XVIII Airborne Corps under Second Army for Plunder (British Batch 2 follow-up); the 43rd Division "Mechelen" row (written as Megchelen); a second British batch (done); the Astonia, Infatuate, Pheasant and Clipper operations (added).
  - `docs/HANDOFF.md`: merging and deleting the handoff and October open-items branches (only `main` exists on GitHub); the list of 27 unfilled US placeholders and the question whether other nations get rows (all done, each batch approved by Scott); the quarterly batch split (both batches approved and merged); where the "Model to 14 September" date came from (settled by the German Batch 2 follow-up: the data never had it).
  - `docs/review/verification_results.md`: the four relations "waiting for Scott" and the list of "new decisions pending" (all decided and applied on 26 October 2026; the decisions themselves stay recorded in the items above them).
  - Kept because still open: the corps that the CMH table entries "X" and "XIV" meant, XXII and XXIII Corps in April and May, the three open German conflict groups, the 243rd Division dissolution row (not approved), combat commands, the commanders table, the outside-review questions, the licence, the 43rd Division's move to VIII Corps on 2 September 1944, and the other open points with their recommendations.
## rule-copyrighted-notes: notes from copyrighted sources stay private (29 October 2026)

Docs and rules only, from `main` (`941b8b0`), with `main` (`d99fc07`) merged in; no data changed. Scott agreed that notes or extracts from copyrighted sources are kept only on the owner's working computer and in his Google Drive (`WWII Unit Locations` folder) and are never committed to the remote repository.

- **`docs/HANDOFF.md`**, section 3: new standing rule 16.
- **`CONTRIBUTING.md`**, Rules: new rule 10.
- **`.gitignore`**: new entries `**/sources/*_notes.md`, `docs/sources/` and `private_notes/`. No tracked file matches them (checked with `git ls-files` and `git check-ignore`).
- No script changed: the sync tool replays only committed files, so ignored files cannot reach the publishing clone.

## french-followup-1944-45: de Lattre pass (29 October 2026)

Same unmerged branch `french-followup-1944-45` (head before the pass `a7113df`; `main` `941b8b0` unchanged). Scott approved using Jean de Lattre de Tassigny, *The History of the French First Army* (trans. Malcolm Barnes, Allen & Unwin 1952; archive.org `bwb_W7-ADK-972`, borrow-only). The book was not re-opened: the evidence is the research notes taken from its OCR text layer (about 335 lines, printed pp. 63-525, gap at p. ~263), each line's quote and printed page. A fact only in a note's paraphrase columns, or only in a truncated or garbled quote, is not used on its own; OCR corrections are stated in the row (`ist` = `1st` in CR0959).

### References and documents

- **`R686`** (new): de Lattre, *The History of the French First Army* (1952); `source_type` `unit_history`, `source_family` `delattre_french_first_army` (its own family, independent of CMH and Wikipedia). Now cited by 6 claims.
- **Research notes**: the notes taken from the book (with a header giving citation, method and known gaps) are kept privately by the project owner (Drive, WWII Unit Locations folder), not in this repository; they were briefly committed on this branch and removed in a later commit. `docs/sources.md`: one row for the source.

### Claims

- **`C1644`** (9th DIC, Toulon, 27 Aug 1944): confidence 0.5 to 0.7. de Lattre pp. 76, 92-93 (St Catherine fort 24 August; Ruhfus's surrender 27 August 23.45) is a second family for the city and day; the quotes do not name the division, the note line does.
- **`C1633`** (5th DB, Stuttgart, 21 Apr 1945): confidence 0.3 to 0.5. de Lattre pp. 460-466: "when Stuttgart fell on the 21st April"; the link of the day to the division rests on the note's summary, so not 0.7.
- **`C1634`** (1st DFL, Lyon, 3 Sep 1944): de Lattre p. 128 (08.00 on 3 September) added as a third family; stays 0.7 (city, 10 km).
- **`C1637`** (2nd DIM, Belfort, 25 Nov 1944): de Lattre pp. 243-246 has Belfort fallen by 21 November; Review note, date and 0.7 unchanged (not reconciled).
- **`C1656`** (new, 3rd DIA, Strasbourg, 5 Jan 1945, 0.5): de Lattre pp. 309, 312 and CMH *Riviera to the Rhine* pp. 517, 522, 527.
- **`C1657`** (new, 1st DB, Ulm, 23 Apr 1945, 0.3): CMH *The Last Offensive* p. 431 (French platoon outside Ulm at dusk) and de Lattre pp. 482, 485-486; attribution to the division inferred. New place `PL0851` Ulm (OpenStreetMap).

### Relations

- **`CR0942`** (2DB under the Seventh Army) and **`CR0953`** (2DB attached to the XV Corps): old valid_to 1945-01-27 (inferred); new 1945-01-18 (narrative): de Lattre p. 373, the combat commands Dio and Langlade left the US Seventh Army on 19 January (two of three; the third is not dated). `C1647` now `asserts_end` for both.
- **`CR0938`** (2DB under the First French Army): old valid_from 1945-01-28 (claim date); new 1945-01-19 (narrative, de Lattre p. 373; `C1648` `asserts_start`).
- **`CR0958`** (new): 2DB under the French II Corps from 28 January 1945 (claim date; CMH p. 548 "leaving Leclerc's 2d Armored Division with de Monsabert"; de Lattre pp. 352, 369-371); end unknown, earliest 8 February. No XXI Corps relation was added: the note for p. 394 puts the XXI Corps link only in its paraphrase and the CMH history leaves the division with the II Corps.
- **`CR0959`** (new): 14th DI under the I Corps from 23 April 1945 (de Lattre p. 479, OCR `ist` read as `1st`; p. 459), asserted by `C1655`.
- **`CR0960`** (new): 3rd DIA under the II Corps from 5 January 1945 (de Lattre p. 312); end inferred (day before the Speyer row), unknown.
- Notes only: `CR0951`, `CR0952` confirmed (de Lattre pp. 286, 287, 302); `CR0946` confirmed (p. 123); `CR0947` kept at 1 September, with de Lattre's "officially went into action at midnight of the 5th to 6th September" (p. 134) recorded; `CR0956` consistent (p. 406).

### Not done (and why)

- 1st DFL to the Alps army detachment from 1 March 1945 (p. 405-406): no 1st DFL row after January 1945 and no place in the quote, so no relation can be anchored; open point.
- I Corps activation (5/6 Sep) and II Corps (1 Sep) as claims: no place in the quotes; recorded on `CR0947`/`CR0946` instead.
- 5th DB at Bregenz (p. 498), 4th DMM at Rouffach (pp. 388-390), 9th DIC at Ensisheim (p. 396), 2nd DB near Obernai-Marlenheim in January (p. 331): the quote does not name the place or the unit, or is truncated.

### Scripts

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** scenario 21 updated (2DB on 1 Feb 1945 now also under the II Corps; C1633 now 0.5); new scenario 22, de Lattre pass: 7 expectations. Total: 344 expectations hold. No other script changed.

## french-followup-1944-45: French follow-up refinements (29 October 2026)

New branch `french-followup-1944-45` from `main` (`941b8b0`, after PR #32 merged the French batch). Scott on 29 October 2026 accepted the seven recommendations of the French batch and asked for a follow-up. Sources: the CMH histories (*Riviera to the Rhine*, *The Last Offensive*), English Wikipedia and, new, French Wikipedia (`R682` to `R684`, same source family as English Wikipedia). De Lattre's *History of the French First Army* (1952) was not read in this first pass (the archive.org copies are borrow-only); it was used later from research notes, see the de Lattre pass above.

### Claims and relations

- **2d French Armored Division, December 1944 to January 1945 (corrected):**
  - The VI Corps relation `CR0937` ran from 27 November 1944 to 7 February 1945 (end inferred from the next row). It now ends on 4 December 1944: the 6th Army Group transferred operational control of the division from the Seventh to the First French Army effective 5 December (*Riviera to the Rhine*, p. 464). Old: valid_to 1945-02-07, inferred, earliest 1944-11-28. New: valid_to 1944-12-04, narrative. The link to `C1616` now asserts both start and end.
  - The Seventh Army relation `CR0942` (standing, unspecified_subordination) ended on 7 February 1945 (inferred). It now ends on 27 January 1945, inferred from the new Grussenheim row (earliest 3 January). It is kept through December because the Seventh Army continued the division's logistical support while the First French Army held operational control (p. 464).
  - The First French Army relation `CR0938` started on 8 February 1945 (the Fessenheim row). It now starts on 28 January 1945, the new Grussenheim row (claim_date); earliest end now 8 February.
  - New `CR0951`: First French Army, operational_control, 5 to 27 December 1944 (p. 464; ended by 6th Army Group Letter of Instructions 7 of 28 December, p. 496, note 7).
  - New `CR0952`: French II Corps, attached, 15 (claim_date) to 27 December 1944 (p. 486).
  - New `CR0953`: XV Corps, attached, 3 January (claim_date) to 27 January 1945 (end inferred from the next row): the division moved to the Sarre valley as XV Corps reserve (p. 501) and supported its counterattacks (p. 505).
  - 3 new claims, `C1646` to `C1648`: the push south from the Selestat region (15 December 1944, representative point, 0.3), the XV Corps sector near Rimling (3 January 1945, date approximate, representative point, 0.3) and Grussenheim (28 January 1945, 0.5).
- **New units (3) and promoted placeholders (2):**
  - `FR-ARMY-ATLANT`, French Forces of the West (Détachement d'Armée de l'Atlantique from March 1945): 1 claim, `C1649`, creation at Cognac on 14 October 1944 (0.5). The two French Wikipedia articles give 1 March and 25 March 1945 for the renaming; noted in the unit row, not reconciled.
  - `FR-INF-GIRONDE`, Gironde marching division (General d'Anselme): 1 claim, `C1650`, operation Vénérable against Royan, 15 April 1945 (0.5). `CR0954`: under the Atlantic detachment, unspecified_subordination, from 15 April (claim_date; the subordination is inferred from the single command of the operation).
  - `FR-INF-027D`, 27th Alpine Infantry Division: 1 claim, `C1651`, the offensive toward the Little St Bernard Pass, 23 March 1945 (representative point, 0.3). `CR0957`: 6th Army Group, unspecified_subordination, from 23 March (claim_date), through General Doyen's Alpine army detachment, which is not a unit.
  - `FR-INF-010D` (placeholder promoted): 2 claims, `C1652` and `C1653`: reconstituted in the Nevers region on 1 October 1944 (0.3) and policing the interior of the Colmar Pocket from 5 February 1945 (CMH p. 551, representative point Colmar, 0.3). `CR0955`: First French Army from 5 February (claim_date).
  - `FR-INF-014D` (placeholder promoted): 2 claims, `C1654` and `C1655`: recreated with its headquarters at Buhl on 9 February 1945 (0.3) and on Lake Constance at the end of the war (8 May 1945, date inferred, 0.3). `CR0956`: First French Army from 9 February (claim_date).
  - Not added: a 1st Alpine Division (no source found), and a division-level La Rochelle force (the sources read describe only a sector under Colonel Chêne and the Oléron brigade).
- **Karlsruhe (`C1639`):** already kept at 3 April 1945 with the CMH 4 April noted; not changed.
- **5th Armored Division at Stuttgart (`C1633`):** date confirmed, 21 April 1945: *The Last Offensive*, p. 429, says the boundary changed on the 20th and a French column broke into Stuttgart the next day; the city was clear on 22 April. The division is not named there, so confidence stays 0.3. Quotes and notes refined; date unchanged.
- **Confidence raised (old 0.5, new 0.7), each annotated in the row:** `C1634` 1st DFL at Lyon, 3 September 1944 (*Riviera to the Rhine*, p. 181); `C1637` 2nd Moroccan Division at Belfort, 25 November 1944 (p. 419); `C1645` 9th Colonial Division at Ensisheim, 6 February 1945 (p. 551; the CMH names the division among the three of the I Corps main effort, so treated as approximate). The other French rows at 0.3 and 0.5 were checked against the CMH chapters; no second source family names the unit at the place, so they are unchanged.
- **Places:** 6 new, `PL0845` to `PL0850`.
- **Claim totals:** 10 new (France), 3 at 0.5 and 7 at 0.3; 4 existing rows changed (`C1633`, `C1634`, `C1637`, `C1645`).
- **Side effect:** the reference tool filled the blank read date (2026-10-27) on `R028` (14th Infantry Division, Wikipedia), as in the French batch.

### Scripts (flag for Scott)

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** new scenario 21, French follow-up: 8 expectations. Total: 337 expectations hold.
- **`validate.py` and the build:** not changed.

### Validator warnings

There are 25 warnings, the same as before. None is on a French row.

## french-1944-45: the French formations (29 October 2026)

New branch `french-1944-45` from `main` (`b021dc2`). It covers the Western Front, 6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945, division level and above; Italy is not covered. Sources: the CMH histories (Blumenson, *Breakout and Pursuit*; Cole, *The Lorraine Campaign*; Clarke and Smith, *Riviera to the Rhine*; MacDonald, *The Last Offensive*) and the Wikipedia unit and battle articles. Five references are new: `R677` (Riviera to the Rhine ch. 30), `R678` (ch. 2), `R679` (ch. 12), `R680` (Wikipedia, 1st Armored Division (France)) and `R681` (Wikipedia, 5th Armored Division (France)). The eleven units were placeholders and are promoted by `promote_placeholders.py`. The nationality folder `data/claims/france` is new. De Lattre's history was not consulted in this pass and is not cited.

### Claims and relations

- **2d French Armored Division (Leclerc):**
  - 12 claims, `C1608` to `C1619`: le Mans (9 August 1944), Ecouche, the Ecouche-Argentan line, Rambouillet, Paris (25 August), Dompaire, north of Rambervillers, Strasbourg (23 November), Erstein, Fessenheim (8 February 1945), Royan (18 April) and Bad Reichenhall (4 May).
  - Corps: `CR0934` XV Corps, attached, 9 to 17 August 1944 (end inferred, earliest 13 August); `CR0935` V Corps, attached, 18 August (first row) to 7 September; `CR0936` XV Corps, attached, 8 September to 26 November (Lorraine p. 185; Riviera p. 454); `CR0937` VI Corps, attached, from 27 November 1944 (end inferred: 7 February 1945, earliest 28 November).
  - Armies (unspecified_subordination, a French division under American command): `CR0939` Third Army, 9 to 17 August 1944 (end inferred); `CR0940` First Army, 18 August to 7 September; `CR0941` Third Army, 8 to 28 September; `CR0942` Seventh Army, from 29 September 1944 (Riviera p. 254; end inferred: 7 February 1945, earliest 28 November); `CR0938` First French Army, from 8 February 1945 (first row; end inferred: 17 April 1945).
  - The Royan and Bad Reichenhall rows have no parent: de Larminat's French Forces of the West are not a unit, and the Last Offensive p. 442 says only "recently attached".
- **First French Army (Army B):** 5 claims, `C1620` to `C1624`: Toulon (20 and 28 August 1944), the activation of the 6th Army Group (15 September, at a representative point, Besancon, 0.3), Karlsruhe (4 April 1945) and Stuttgart (22 April). Relations: `CR0943` Seventh Army, unspecified_subordination, 20 August (first row) to 14 September 1944; `CR0944` Seventh Army, operational_control, 15 to 19 September (Riviera p. 224: Patch directed First French Army operations until 19 September); `CR0945` 6th Army Group, assigned, from 15 September 1944, open.
- **French II Corps:** 2 claims, `C1625` and `C1626`: the contact near Bains-les-Bains (17 September 1944) and the Rhine crossing at Speyer and Germersheim (31 March 1945). `CR0946` First French Army, assigned, from 1 September 1944 (Riviera p. 198, note 9).
- **French I Corps:** 3 claims, `C1627` to `C1629`: Aix-en-Provence (1 September 1944), the Colmar Pocket offensive (20 January 1945, representative point Cernay, 0.3) and Chalampe (9 February 1945). `CR0947` First French Army, assigned, from 1 September 1944 (Wikipedia).
- **Divisions:** 16 claims, `C1630` to `C1645`: 1st Armored Division 3 (Delle, Rosenau on the Rhine on 19 November 1944, Mulhouse); 5th Armored Division 1 (Stuttgart, 21 April 1945, date inferred, 0.3); 1st DFL/1st DMI 2 (Lyon, the Colmar Pocket); 2nd Moroccan 4 (Briancon, Belfort, Germersheim, Karlsruhe); 3rd Algerian 2 (Marseille, Speyer); 4th Moroccan Mountain 2 (Rouffach, the Vorarlberg at a representative point, Bregenz); 9th Colonial 2 (Toulon, Ensisheim). Relations to the I Corps, unspecified_subordination: `CR0948` 1st Armored Division from 18 November 1944 (first row), `CR0949` 4th Moroccan Mountain Division 5 February to 1 May 1945 (end inferred), `CR0950` 9th Colonial Division from 6 February 1945.
- **Places:** 18 new, `PL0827` to `PL0844`.
- **Claim totals:** 38 (France). Confidence: 5 at 0.9, 13 at 0.7, 15 at 0.5, 5 at 0.3.

### Inferred values (each also in the record note)

- Representative points (0.3 or a wider precision, location_basis representative_point): the Ecouche-Argentan line (Ecouche), the front north of Rambervillers, the army on 15 September 1944 (Besancon), the II Corps contact (Bains-les-Bains) and crossing (Speyer), the I Corps attack of 20 January 1945 (Cernay), the 1st DFL attack of 23 January 1945 (Selestat) and the 4th Moroccan Mountain Division in the Vorarlberg (Bregenz).
- The 5th Armored Division row at Stuttgart is dated 21 April 1945 from an unnamed French column in the CMH history; Wikipedia gives only April 1945.
- Relation starts with basis claim_date and ends with basis inferred_from_next_claim are listed above; each note gives the reason.
- No existing claim or relation was changed. The reference tool filled the blank read date of the existing Wikipedia references that the batch cites (`R038`, `R048`, `R053`, `R061`, `R070`, `R104`, `R119`, `R129`, `R134` and others) with 2026-10-27, its fixed value, and updated their claim counts.

### Scripts (flag for Scott)

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** new scenario 20, French batch: 12 expectations. Total: 329 expectations hold.
- **`validate.py` and the build:** not changed.

### Validator warnings

There are 25 warnings, the same as before the batch. None is on a French row.

## allied-contingents-1944-45: the Polish, Czechoslovak, Belgian and Dutch formations (29 October 2026)

New branch `allied-contingents-1944-45` from `main` (`85c5c48`). It covers the Western Front, 6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945; Italy is not covered. Sources: Stacey (`R668`), Ellis vol. I and II (`R670`, `R671`) and the Wikipedia unit articles (`R035`, `R037`, `R039`, `R144`), plus two new Wikipedia references: `R675` (Siege of Dunkirk) and `R676` (Princess Irene Brigade). The five units were placeholders and are promoted by `promote_placeholders.py`. Four nationality folders are new under `data/claims/`: `poland`, `czechoslovakia`, `belgium` and `netherlands`. Each unit is its own commit.

### Claims and relations

- **1st Polish Armoured Division:**
  - 33 claims, `C1547` to `C1579`: 8 August 1944 (Totalize) to 6 May 1945 (Wilhelmshaven). Confidence: 9 at 0.9, 20 at 0.7, 3 at 0.5, 1 at 0.3.
  - Uses the existing `CR0853` (II Canadian Corps, 5 August to 28 September 1944).
  - New `CR0923`: I Corps, attached, from 28 September 1944. The end is inferred: 7 April 1945, earliest 7 January. Ellis p. 285 has the division under Netherlands District, which is not a unit, and the change is undated.
  - New `CR0924`: II Canadian Corps, attached, from 8 April 1945, open.
  - New `CR0925`: First Canadian Army, unspecified_subordination, from 5 August 1944 (inferred from Stacey p. 208), open (Stacey p. 618: it left Crerar's command in mid-June 1945).
- **Polish Parachute Brigade:**
  - 5 claims, `C1580` to `C1584`: Oosterbeek (gliders), Driel (the drop, the crossing and Horrocks's visit) and Nijmegen, 19 to 26 September 1944.
  - New `CR0926`: 1st Airborne Division, attached, 17 to 26 September 1944. Stacey p. 313 says "under command". Both dates are inferred.
- **Czechoslovak Armoured Brigade Group:**
  - 5 claims, `C1585` to `C1589`: Falaise on 30 August 1944, then Dunkirk on 9 October, 28 October, 27 November and 8 May 1945. The last date is representative, at 0.3.
  - New `CR0927`: 21st Army Group, unspecified_subordination, from 30 August 1944, open.
  - New `CR0928`: First Canadian Army, attached, 9 October to 27 November 1944 (Stacey p. 368).
- **Belgian (Piron) Brigade:**
  - 10 claims, `C1590` to `C1599`: the Normandy coast, Brussels, Leopoldsburg, Thorn and Leuven, then Opheusden on 18 April 1945.
  - New `CR0929`: First Canadian Army, 13 August to 1 September 1944 (Stacey pp. 265 and 323).
  - New `CR0930`: Second Army, from 2 September 1944. The end is inferred: 11 April 1945, earliest 17 November 1944.
  - New `CR0931`: I Canadian Corps, from 12 April 1945 (Stacey p. 570; the 1st Canadian Armoured Brigade is not a unit). All three are unspecified_subordination.
- **Princess Irene (Royal Netherlands) Brigade:**
  - 8 claims, `C1600` to `C1607`: Graye-sur-Mer, the Normandy coast, Beringen, Borkel, the Nijmegen bridges (Ellis II p. 42, note), Grave, Broekhoven (Pheasant) and Hedel on 25 April 1945, which has no parent.
  - New `CR0932`: First Canadian Army, 13 August to 1 September 1944.
  - New `CR0933`: Second Army, 2 September 1944 to 1 March 1945. The end is inferred: Wikipedia says Netherlands District took over on 2 March.
- **Places:** 41 new, `PL0786` to `PL0826`.
- **Claim totals:** 61 (Poland 38, Belgium 10, Netherlands 8, Czechoslovakia 5). Confidence: 11 at 0.9, 26 at 0.7, 19 at 0.5, 5 at 0.3.

### Changed values on existing records (old value, new value, reason; each also in the record note)

- **`CR0892` (Belgian brigade under the 6th Airborne Division):**
  - Start: 1944-08-21 (basis claim_date, inferred) changed to 1944-08-13 (basis narrative), because Stacey p. 265 dates the start.
  - End: blank (open_at_last_claim, end unknown) changed to 1944-08-27 (narrative, end_unknown false), because Wikipedia places the brigade under the 49th Division on 28 August.
  - date_basis: inferred changed to narrative.
- **`CR0893` (Princess Irene Brigade under the 6th Airborne Division):**
  - Start: 1944-08-21 changed to 1944-08-13 (narrative), because Stacey p. 265 dates the start.
  - End: blank (open) changed to 1944-09-01 (inferred_from_next_claim, earliest 1944-08-21), because the brigade passed to the Second Army on 2 September.
- **`C1568`:** its link to `CR0923` was removed, because the row (8 April 1945) is after that relation's inferred end. The semantic staleness test caught it.

### Scripts (flag for Scott)

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:**
  - New scenario 19, Allied contingents: 16 expectations.
  - Scenario 16 changed. The Belgian brigade on 25 August 1944 was expected to have the 6th Airborne Division only; it now also has First Canadian Army (unspecified_subordination), from the new relation `CR0929`.
  - Total: 317 expectations hold.
- **`validate.py` and the build:** not changed.

### Validator warnings

There are 25 warnings, 20 before. The 5 new ones are on the Polish Parachute Brigade rows: "attached entry without a standing parent". No source read names a standing parent such as I Airborne Corps, and each row's Relationship note says so.

## british-batch2-jan-may-1945, follow-up: the units not covered, and the XVIII Airborne Corps (28 October 2026)

Follow-up commits on the same branch `british-batch2-jan-may-1945` (Scott on 28 October 2026 accepted the Batch 2 recommendations, including the one-line `validate.py` change, and approved a deeper pass; the branch was intact on `origin` at `fbc3875`, not merged; no new branch, no force-push). Sources beyond Ellis: Stacey (`R668`), MacDonald, *The Last Offensive* ch. 14 (`R543`) and ch. 16 (`R566`), and two pages of Stichting Opdat Wij Niet Vergeten (`R673`, `R674`, ownv.nl). Nafziger (generalstaff.org) is blocked to the box by Cloudflare (403); only search snippets were reachable and nothing from it is cited.

### Task 1, claims and relations (each item its own commit)

- **50th Division (`C1539`, `C1540`, `C1541`; `CR0918`; places `PL0782` Neerpelt, `PL0783` Elst):** 17 September 1944 at Neerpelt (0.3: Ellis vol. II p. 32 places only the heavy mortars and one battalion at the bridgehead), 1 October on the Island (0.5; the date is the first day of the attacks, inferred; Ellis p. 98 names no place and Elst is a representative point), 9 November on the Island under II Canadian Corps (0.5; Stacey pp. 428, 430; Ellis p. 159). `CR0918` attaches the division to II Canadian Corps from 9 November (narrative) to 29 November (stated by `C1541`: Ellis p. 159 "On the 29th it was moved back to Belgium", month inferred as November from Stacey p. 430 and Ellis p. 376). Commander of `C1541` is `unknown` (Stacey names Graham, Ellis lists Lyne). The 50th has no row for 29 November or December 1944 ("Belgium" is not a place).
- **3rd Division (`C1542`, `C1543`; `CR0919`; place `PL0784` Well):** 19 January 1945, the Maas front between Venlo and Boxmeer, last day under II Canadian Corps (0.5, precision 25 km; Stacey p. 443, Ellis pp. 238, 240); 25 February at Weeze, relieving the 15th Division in XXX Corps (0.5; Stacey p. 494, Ellis pp. 271, 273). New `CR0919`: 3rd Division in VIII Corps from 3 September 1944 (Ellis vol. II p. 4, note) to 24 February 1945 (end inferred from the next row).
- **43rd Division (`C1544`; place `PL0785` Megchelen; references `R673`, `R674`):** 28 March 1945, 0.7. The "Mechelen" of Ellis p. 293 is the Dutch village of Megchelen (identification from Ellis's sequence and two ownv.nl pages that give the 43rd (Wessex) Division in Megchelen on the morning of 28 March); the row says so in its Context note.
- **49th Division (`C1545`; `CR0920`):** 4 December 1944 on the Island (0.5, Stacey pp. 430, 433, one family). `CR0920` attaches the division to II Canadian Corps from 30 November (start read from "the end of November", latest 2 December) to 14 March 1945 (inferred; I Canadian Corps takes the division at noon on 15 March, Stacey p. 545).
- **59th Division (no claim):** `CR0882` (XII Corps) closed on 23 August 1944, earliest 6 August, basis `inferred_from_next_claim` (Ellis vol. I p. 453: XII Corps "had to shed the 59th Division, unhappily selected for disbandment" and is well on its way by the 24th). No place, so no row.
- **Not written, with reasons:** the 1st Airborne Division (no place and date after Arnhem in the sources read), the 79th Armoured Division (specialist formation; no division-level place or headquarters), the 59th (above), the 50th on 29 November and in December 1944, and any parent for `C1482` (it stays blank; the relations carry the chain).

### Task 2, the one US claim (own commit)

- **`C1546` (US-ABN-018C, 24 March 1945, 0.3, place `PL0437` Diersfordt, parent `UK-ARMY-002A`, `OP-VARSITY`):** the XVIII Airborne Corps controlling the 6th British and 17th US Airborne Divisions for Plunder and Varsity (Ellis vol. II p. 285; MacDonald ch. 14 pp. 298-300, 309, 310; ch. 16 pp. 357, 362, 365). The point is the corps objective (the Diersfordter Forst), not the headquarters (on the west bank of the Rhine, no place named), so 0.3.
- **`CR0921`:** XVIII Airborne Corps attached to Second Army from 9 March ("As of 9 March", CMH ch. 14 p. 298) to 1 April 1945 (stated by `C1546` from ch. 16 pp. 357, 362; read as the day before `CR0922`).
- **`CR0922`:** XVIII Airborne Corps under the US First Army (operational control) from 2 April 1945 (latest 5 April; CMH ch. 16 p. 362, the corps attacks under First Army on 6 April, p. 365), open. It continues `CR0085` after the Second Army attachment. `C1546` states its start, which gives one expected validator warning (the start is after the row date; see the row's Context note).

### Changed values on existing records (old value, new value, reason; each in the record note)

- **`CR0070` (3rd Division in I Corps):** valid_to 1945-02-28 becomes 1944-09-02 (earliest 1944-07-18 unchanged). Ellis vol. II pp. 4, 159, 238 have the division in VIII Corps from 3 September 1944; the day of the change is not dated.
- **`CR0905` (3rd Division in XXX Corps):** valid_from 1945-03-01 becomes 1945-02-25 (Stacey p. 494; Ellis p. 271).
- **`CR0862` (3rd Division attached to II Canadian Corps):** note only; the sentence that the division stays assigned to I Corps is replaced by VIII Corps (`CR0919`).
- **`CR0882` (59th Division in XII Corps):** no end becomes valid_to 1944-08-23 (above).
- **`CR0085` (XVIII Airborne Corps under First Army):** no end becomes valid_to 1945-03-08 (earliest 1944-12-19), because the corps was attached to Second Army from 9 March.
- **`CR0909` and `C1508` (6th Airborne Division, notes only):** the chain on 24 March 1945 changes from XVIII Airborne Corps, US First Army, 12th Army Group, SHAEF to XVIII Airborne Corps, British Second Army (`CR0921`), 21st Army Group, SHAEF (CMH ch. 14 p. 298; Ellis p. 285). CMH ch. 16 p. 357 plans for the British units to pass to Second Army at the end of March, which may end `CR0909` earlier than 16 April; the end is left as it is and says so.
- **`C1482` (49th Division, Zetten, 18 January 1945):** the row is unchanged; links to `CR0888` (I Corps, standing) and `CR0920` (II Canadian Corps) added, with a note.

### Tests, checks and counts

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`**: scenario 18 added (17 expectations: the 3rd Division in VIII Corps, attached to II Canadian Corps and in XXX Corps from 25 February; the 50th and 49th Divisions attached to II Canadian Corps; the 59th Division in XII Corps until 23 August; the XVIII Airborne Corps under Second Army on 24 March and under First Army on 10 April; the 43rd Division in XXX Corps on 28 March; the first and last row of the follow-up). 301 expectations in all.
- **Counts:** 8 claims (`C1539` to `C1546`; 1 at 0.7, 5 at 0.5, 2 at 0.3), 5 relations (`CR0918` to `CR0922`), 15 evidence links added (2,304 in all), 4 places (`PL0782` to `PL0785`), 2 references (`R673`, `R674`; 668 in all), 7 existing relations and 2 claims changed (`CR0070`, `CR0085`, `CR0862`, `CR0882`, `CR0905`, `CR0909`; `C1482` links, `C1508` note). Next free ids: `C1547`, `CR0923`, `PL0786`, `R675`, `G028`.
- **Checks:** `validate` OK (264 claim files, 1,546 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups; 20 warnings: the 19 known and the `C1546` one above); `semantic_tests` OK (301 expectations); the full set as listed in the HANDOFF. who_controls grids before and after: 214 of 10,860 answers changed in the standard grid, 138 of 2,469 in the extended grid (12 March to 7 May 1945) and 207 of 2,958 in a new grid for 1 January to 11 March 1945; all explained in `grid_british_batch2.md` (follow-up section). No new keys. The temporal audit gains one `overlap` finding and one `start_disagree` finding, both for the XVIII Airborne Corps and both artefacts of the split of the First Army relation into `CR0085` (to 8 March) and `CR0922` (from 2 April): `C1546` lists both relations, so the audit reads a First Army listing on 24 March beside the Second Army attachment; the command-location audit has 1,546 claims and no finding on the new rows.
- **Documents:** README counts and a sentence; `docs/HANDOFF.md` (state table, baselines, decision log); `docs/roadmap.md` (table row and section); `docs/sources.md`; `docs/coverage.md`, `docs/review/` and `build/` regenerated.

## british-batch2-jan-may-1945: British Batch 2, 22 October 1944 to 4 May 1945 (28 October 2026)

Branch `british-batch2-jan-may-1945`, cut from the British Batch 1 head (`9b4c775`, 1,468 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups, 262 semantic expectations); compare it with `main` (`https://github.com/scottstarsman/UnitLocationData/compare/main...british-batch2-jan-may-1945`). Task: British (UK) Army units on the Western Front, 1 January to 8 May 1945, division level and above; same method, scope, sources and rules as British Batch 1 and Canadian Batch 2 (Ellis, *Victory in the West* vol. II, `R671`, with Stacey, `R668`, for the five operations); Scott on 28 October 2026: "Let's start British 45".

### Item 1: operations, a reference and six re-pointed rows

- **Reference `R672` (`data/references_meta.csv`, regenerated into `data/references.csv`):** Wikipedia, Operation Pheasant (end date and the name only; Ellis does not name it).
- **Operations `OP-ASTONIA` (10 to 12 September 1944), `OP-INFATUATE` (1 to 8 November 1944), `OP-PHEASANT` (20 October to 4 November 1944), `OP-BLACKCOCK` (16 to 26 January 1945), `OP-PLUNDER` (23 to 27 March 1945)** appended to `data/operations.csv`, with `operation_relations` rows `OP-VARSITY` supports `OP-PLUNDER`, `OP-PHEASANT` supports `OP-SCHELDT`, `OP-INFATUATE` supports `OP-SCHELDT` (a battle may not be the parent of an operation, so `OP-INFATUATE` has the campaign `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND` as parent). The end of Plunder is inferred and says so in `notes`. `OP-CLIPPER` already existed.
- **`C1459`, `C1460`, `C1461` (British Batch 1, Le Havre), column `operation_id`: changed.** Old value: `OP-CAMP-NORTHERN-FRANCE`. New value: `OP-ASTONIA`. Reason: Stacey p. 331 names Astonia as the three-day operation against Le Havre in the second week of September 1944.
- **`C1329` (`CAN-INF-002D`), `C1467` (`UK-INF-052D`), `C1036` (`GER-INF-070D`), column `operation_id`: changed.** Old value: `OP-SCHELDT`. New value: `OP-INFATUATE`. Reason: all three describe the Walcheren assault of 1 to 8 November (Stacey p. 407). `C1283` (8 November, Canadian Army) stays under `OP-SCHELDT`. A `Review (British Batch 2, 28 October 2026; ...)` sentence in each note gives the old value, the new value and the reason; a Stacey reference was added to each.

### Items 2 to 6: claims and relations

- **Claims `C1469` to `C1538` (70):** 1944 (flagged additions to Batch 1, `C1469` to `C1472`: XII Corps on 22 October and the 53rd Division on 27 October for Pheasant, XXX Corps and the 43rd Division on 18 November for Clipper); January 1945 `C1473` to `C1482` (the Ardennes counter-attack at Marche, Bure and La Roche, Blackcock from 16 January, 49th Division at Zetten); February and March `C1483` to `C1503` (Veritable, Goch, Udem, Kervenheim, Weeze, Alpen, Blockbuster, the end of the Rhineland battle); the Rhine crossing `C1504` to `C1510` (Plunder and Varsity); April and May `C1511` to `C1538` (Lingen, Osnabruck and Hannover, Celle and Belsen, Bremen, the Elbe at Lauenburg, Luebeck, Wismar, Travemuende, Hamburg and the surrender on Lueneburg Heath). Confidence 0.7 for 45, 0.5 for 22, 0.3 for 3 (`C1483` and `C1502`, XXX Corps headquarters on 7 February and 8 March, and `C1511`, Second Army on 28 March; all three are representative points), all from Ellis, one family.
- **By unit:** 15th Division 8, XXX Corps 7, 53rd Division 7, 11th Armoured 6, 43rd Division 6, 52nd Division 6, 51st Division 5, 7th Armoured 5, 6th Airborne 4, Guards Armoured 4, XII Corps 3, 3rd Division 3, 21st Army Group 2, VIII Corps 2, 49th Division 1, Second Army 1. No unit was activated (all were already active).
- **Inferred values (all annotated in the claim or relation notes):** the latest-possible days (`C1495` and `C1503`: the 52nd Division's clearing of Alpen by 9 March, Ellis gives no earlier day), the representative points (`C1483`, `C1502` and `C1511`, the Hamburg objective), the 6th Airborne Division attached to XVIII Airborne Corps from 24 March to 16 April (the end is inferred from the VIII Corps row of 8 April), the closings of `CR0905` and `CR0863` and `CR0903` (below), and `C1538` (the signature is at Lueneburg Heath; the point is the Timeloberg hill of the existing place `PL0643`).
- **New relations `CR0894` to `CR0917` (24):** the XXX, XII and VIII Corps standing relations of the divisions in the order Ellis gives them, the attachments of the 52nd and 11th Armoured Divisions to First Canadian Army (`CR0902`, `CR0904`) and of the 11th Armoured Division to II Canadian Corps (`CR0903`), and the attachment of the 6th Airborne Division to XVIII Airborne Corps (`CR0909` from 24 March to 16 April and `CR0917` from 2 May). The 21st Army Group relation to SHAEF (`CR0057`) is re-evidenced by `C1537` and `C1538` (no new relation).
- **Existing relations closed as inferred values (`valid_to` was empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`; new `valid_to`, `valid_to_earliest`, basis `inferred_from_next_claim`; the relation note carries the old value, the new value and the reason):** `CR0055` (6th Airborne under I Corps, 1945-01-02, earliest 1944-08-26), `CR0070` (3rd Division under I Corps, 1945-02-28), `CR0078` (51st Division under I Corps, 1945-01-10), `CR0876` (53rd Division under XII Corps, 1945-01-02), `CR0880` (43rd Division under XXX Corps, 1945-01-23), `CR0884` (11th Armoured Division under XXX Corps, 1945-04-07), `CR0885` (15th Division under XII Corps, 1945-02-07), `CR0886` (Guards Armoured under XXX Corps, 1945-04-26), `CR0891` (52nd Division attached to First Canadian Army, 1945-01-17, earliest 1944-11-03) and `CR0863` (43rd Division attached to II Canadian Corps, 1945-03-22, earliest 1945-03-08: the Ellis p. 285 order of battle puts it in XXX Corps on 23 March). Three relations created in this batch were closed the same way (`CR0903` 1945-03-22 for the same reason, with the 11th Armoured Division in VIII Corps (Army Reserve) in Ellis p. 285, and `CR0905`, `CR0902`, `CR0904` by the next row). The reason in each case is that the unit's next row names another higher unit and the sources read do not date the change, so the day before that row is used.
- **Added to `data/command_relation_claims.csv`:** 93 evidence links (of which 12 are `listed_by_higher_unit` links from `C1471`, `C1504`, `C1505`, `C1517` and `C1530` for the divisions of a corps; those starting before the first direct claim state `effective_from` and the relation has basis narrative).
- **Places:** 33 new, `PL0749` to `PL0781` (geocoded with Nominatim, place centres; precision 3 to 25 km), including 's-Hertogenbosch, Bure, La Roche-en-Ardenne, Sittard, Susteren, Goch, Weeze, Alpen, Hamminkeln, Brünen, Enschede, Lingen, Celle, Bergen-Belsen, Lueneburg, Luebeck, Travemuende, Wismar and Hamburg.
- **Not written (stated scope):** the 1st Airborne Division, the 50th, 59th Divisions and the 79th Armoured Division (no place and date in Ellis for the period), the 3rd Division in January and February, the 43rd Division at "Mechelen" (place uncertain), I Corps in 1945 (the Canadian batches cover it); Polish, Czech, Belgian and Dutch units; independent and armoured brigades; the 243rd Division German row.

### Item 7: tests and validator

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`**: scenario 17 added (22 expectations: 52nd, 53rd, 43rd and 11th Armoured Divisions and the 6th Airborne Division under their corps and attachments, the 15th and 3rd Divisions, XXX Corps with and without the First Canadian Army, the operation of the six re-pointed or new rows, the ids `C1469` and `C1538`).
- **`scripts/validate.py` (one line):** `valid_to_earliest` of an inferred end must be the date of a linked claim; the test read only the unit's own claims when there were any, and now also the higher-unit claims that list the relation (`lc_all`). Reason: the semantic test of scenario 0 requires `last_listed_date` (which counts the listing claims) to equal `valid_to_earliest`, so `CR0863` (listed by `C1353`) and `CR0905` (listed by `C1505`) could satisfy only one of the two checks. Nothing else in the validator changed; all earlier rows still pass.
- **`wc_grid` / `cmp_grid`:** before the Batch 1 head `9b4c775`, after the branch: 1,178 of 10,860 answers changed, no new keys; an extended grid for 12 March to 7 May 1945 (9 weekly dates, three units daily) changed 503 of 2,469 answers (see `grid_british_batch2.md`).

### Item 8: documents

- **Counts:** 70 claims (`C1469` to `C1538`), 24 relations (`CR0894` to `CR0917`), 93 evidence links, 33 places, 5 operations (67 in all), 3 operation relations, 1 reference (666 in all), 6 existing claims re-pointed, 10 existing relations closed. Next free ids: `C1539`, `CR0918`, `PL0782`, `R673`, `G028`.
- **Checks:** `validate` OK (264 claim files, 1,538 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups; 19 warnings, 3 coordinate warnings); `semantic_tests` OK (284 expectations); the full set as listed in the HANDOFF.
- **Documents:** README counts and a British Batch 2 sentence; `docs/HANDOFF.md` (state table, baselines, decision log); `docs/roadmap.md` (table row and section); `docs/sources.md` (Ellis pages); `docs/coverage.md`, `docs/review/` and `build/` regenerated.

## british-batch1-jun-dec-1944: British Batch 1, 6 June to 31 December 1944 (28 October 2026)

Branch `british-batch1-jun-dec-1944`, cut from `main` (`c89feaa`, 1,407 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups, 243 semantic expectations); compare it with `main` (`https://github.com/scottstarsman/UnitLocationData/compare/main...british-batch1-jun-dec-1944`). Scott on 28 October 2026: "I think the British units should be next. Pick the time period that works best for you and proceed"; the period 6 June to 31 December 1944 was chosen and written in one batch.

### Item 1: references and operations

- **References `R669`, `R670`, `R671` (`data/references_meta.csv`, regenerated into `data/references.csv`):** Wikipedia, Operation Bluecoat; Ellis, *Victory in the West* vol. I (HMSO 1962; archive.org full text); Ellis vol. II (HMSO 1968).
- **Operations `OP-TRACTABLE` (14 to 21 August 1944, parent `OP-FALAISE`) and `OP-BLUECOAT` (30 July to 7 August, parent `OP-CAMP-NORTHERN-FRANCE`)** appended to `data/operations.csv`. Tractable is the Canadian Batch 1 deferred item; the end follows the closing of the Falaise Gap and the Bluecoat end and name come from Wikipedia (Ellis does not name it); both are inferred values and say so in `notes`.

### Item 2: three Canadian rows re-pointed (changed values)

- **`C1291` (`CAN-COR-002C`), `C1303` (`CAN-INF-003D`), `C1334` (`CAN-ARM-004D`), column `operation_id`: changed.** Old value: `OP-FALAISE`. New value: `OP-TRACTABLE`. Reason: all three describe 14 August 1944 (the first day of Tractable, Stacey pp. 238-241 and Ellis I p. 430); the other Canadian Falaise rows were not changed. A `Review (British Batch 1, 28 October 2026; ...)` sentence in each note gives the old value, the new value and the reason.

### Items 3 to 6: claims and relations

- **Claims `C1408` to `C1468` (61):** July `C1408` to `C1433` (26: Charnwood, Jupiter, the Odon preliminaries, Goodwood, Bluecoat); August `C1434` to `C1456` (23: Bluecoat and Mont Pincon, 21st Army Group and XXX Corps command changes of 4 August, Tractable flank, the Falaise pursuit, the Seine crossings, Amiens and the Somme, Montgomery promoted Field-Marshal on 1 September); September to December `C1457` to `C1468` (12: Brussels and Antwerp, Le Havre, Market Garden with I Airborne Corps and the 1st Airborne Division, the 52nd Division at Walcheren, XXX Corps in the Ardennes). Confidence 0.7 for 40, 0.5 for 19, 0.3 for 2 (`C1423`, `C1456`); all from Ellis, one family.
- **Inferred values (all annotated in the claim or relation notes):** the claim dates are the days Ellis gives except `C1443` (6th Airborne at Troarn: Ellis I p. 448 gives no day; 21 August is the latest possible day), `C1468` (20 December is the day Montgomery took control of the northern battlefield; the order is earlier), `C1466` (the withdrawal from Arnhem at night, 25-26 September, Ellis II pp. 58 and 97 differ by a day), the closing of `CR0883` (29 August, Ellis I p. 467) and the start of `CR0891` (52nd Division attached to the First Canadian Army, claim date 3 November; Ellis II p. 106 has a brigade relieving Canadians from 18 October).
- **New relations `CR0873` to `CR0893` (21):** I Corps, XII Corps, VIII Corps, XXX Corps and Second Army standing relations of the divisions and corps (assigned, from the first British row in the new place), `CR0883` (7th Armoured Division attached to I Corps from 16 August to 29 August), `CR0888` (49th Division assigned to I Corps from 10 September), two attached relations of the Belgian and Netherlands brigades to the 6th Airborne Division (listed by `C1443`), the 1st Airborne Division, I Airborne Corps and the 52nd Division relations.
- **`CR0060`, `CR0063`, `CR0072`, `CR0074`, `CR0076`: closed as inferred values.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`. New value: `valid_to` the day before the first row of the next higher unit (for example `CR0060` 1944-07-18), basis `inferred_from_next_claim`. Reason: the unit's next row names another corps and the sources read do not date the change; the relation note carries the same text ("The old value is ... The new value is ... The reason is ...").
- **`CR0850` and `CR0851` (7th Armoured and Guards Armoured Divisions attached to II Canadian Corps from 20 July): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`, end unknown. New value: `valid_to` 1944-07-30, `valid_to_earliest` 1944-07-25, basis `inferred_from_next_claim`, end unknown. Reason: Ellis I p. 378 has both divisions attacking with II Canadian Corps on 25 July and p. 388 puts them in XXX Corps and VIII Corps reserve for Bluecoat on 30 July; the day of the end is not dated, so the day of the next operation is the latest possible end.
- **Added to `data/command_relation_claims.csv`:** 64 evidence links. `CR0883` is evidenced by the I Corps row `C1442` through `listed_by_higher_unit` (`composing_units` of the 7th Armoured Division, start stated 16 August) and the two brigade relations by `C1443`.
- **Places:** 27 new, `PL0722` to `PL0748` (geocoded with Nominatim, place centres; precision 3 to 20 km), including Mont Pincon, Evrecy, Aunay-sur-Odon, Vernon, Amiens, Brussels, Valkenswaard, Wolfheze and Tienen.
- **Units activated (`data/units.csv`, `record_status` placeholder to active):** `UK-COR-012C` (XII Corps), `UK-ABN-001C` (I Airborne Corps), `UK-ABN-001D` (1st Airborne Division), `UK-INF-052D` (52nd Division).
- **Not written (stated scope):** the Polish Armoured Division to I Corps from 28 September (Stacey p. 367), the Polish Parachute Brigade at Arnhem and the Czechoslovak brigade at Dunkirk (no claim of those units exists to evidence the relations); the 43rd Division's move to VIII Corps on 2 September; the 243rd Division German row (deferred earlier); October 1944 to May 1945 for the British formations.

### Item 7: tests

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`**: scenario 16 added (19 expectations: the end of the II Canadian Corps attachments of the 7th Armoured and Guards Armoured Divisions, the 7th Armoured Division in XXX Corps, I Corps (attached) and XII Corps, XII Corps under Second Army, the 43rd, 59th, 15th, 49th Divisions and the 11th Armoured Division under their corps, the 1st Airborne Division under I Airborne Corps and First Allied Airborne Army, the Belgian Brigade attached to the 6th Airborne Division, the 52nd Division attached to First Canadian Army, and the ids `C1408` and `C1468`).
- **`wc_grid` / `cmp_grid`:** before `main` `c89feaa`, after the branch: 1,550 of 10,620 answers changed, 240 new questions (6 units); the US changes are only the date of the last listing of the SHAEF relation (see `grid_british_batch1.md`).

### Item 8: documents

- **Counts:** 61 claims (`C1408` to `C1468`), 21 relations, 64 evidence links, 4 units activated, 27 places, 2 operations (62 in all), 3 references (665 in all). Next free ids: `C1469`, `CR0894`, `PL0749`, `R672`, `G028`.
- **Checks:** `validate` OK (264 claim files, 1,468 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups; 18 warnings, 3 coordinate warnings); `semantic_tests` OK (262 expectations); the full set (`build`, `build_references`, `review_sample --check`, `check_coordinates`, both audits, `check_claim_ids --base origin/main`, `run_checks.sh`) as listed in the HANDOFF.
- **Documents:** README counts and a British Batch 1 sentence; `docs/HANDOFF.md` (state table, baselines, decision log); `docs/roadmap.md` (table row and section); `docs/sources.md` (Ellis); `docs/coverage.md` and `build/` regenerated.

## canadian-batch2-jan-may-1945: Canadian Batch 2, January to May 1945 (28 October 2026)

Branch `canadian-batch2-jan-may-1945`, cut from `main` (`92e5f82`, 1,342 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups, 221 semantic expectations); compare it with `main` (`https://github.com/scottstarsman/UnitLocationData/compare/main...canadian-batch2-jan-may-1945`). Task approved by Scott on 28 October 2026 (Canadian Batch 2: Canadian Army units on the Western Front, 1 January to 8 May 1945, division level and above; same method, scope, sources and rules as Canadian Batch 1; the 2nd Division at Boxtel of 29 December 1944 as a deferred first row). One source family only (Stacey, `R668`), so no row is at 0.9. Italy is not written: I Canadian Corps, the 1st Division and the 5th Armoured Division appear only from their arrival in the Western Front theatre.

### Item 1: operations and the scope of R668

- **Three operations** appended to `data/operations.csv`: `OP-ELEPHANT` (26 to 31 January 1945, parent `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`), `OP-VERITABLE` (8 February to 10 March, parent `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`; the end follows the casualty period of Stacey p. 522, no single closing day is named) and `OP-BLOCKBUSTER` (phase, 26 February to 10 March, parent `OP-VERITABLE`; the end follows Veritable, Stacey names no separate end). One row in `data/operation_relations.csv`: `OP-VERITABLE` concurrent with `OP-GRENADE`.
- **`R668` (`data/references.csv`), column `used_for`: changed.** Old value: the text ending "6 June 1944 to 31 December 1944" (scope as of Canadian Batch 1). New value: the same text ending "6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945". Reason: Stacey is now used for January to May 1945.

### Items 2 to 4: claims and relations

- **Claims `C1343` to `C1407` (65):** First Canadian Army `C1343` to `C1348` (6); II Canadian Corps `C1349` to `C1360` (12); I Canadian Corps `C1361` to `C1366` (6, from 15 March); 2nd Division `C1367` to `C1378` (12, including `C1367`, the deferred row of 29 December 1944: the 4th Brigade Group only, at Boxtel, confidence 0.3); 3rd Division `C1379` to `C1388` (10); 4th Armoured Division `C1389` to `C1396` (8); 1st Division `C1397` to `C1401` (5, from 3 April); 5th Armoured Division `C1402` to `C1407` (6, from the last days of February). Confidence: 0.7 for 27, 0.5 for 26, 0.3 for 12. New claim files `CAN-COR-001C.csv`, `CAN-INF-001D.csv`, `CAN-ARM-005D.csv`; the placeholders `CAN-COR-001C`, `CAN-INF-001D`, `CAN-ARM-005D` became active (260 active, 107 placeholders). Commanders from Appendix G (Crerar, Simonds, Foulkes, Matthews, Spry then Keefler, Vokes, Foster, Hoffmeister).
- **Inferred values (all annotated in the claim or relation notes):** `C1402` is dated 28 February 1945 for the last days of February (Stacey p. 529; the day is inferred); the start of `CR0866` (3rd Division attached to the 30th Corps) is inferred as 8 February from the opening of the division part at 18:00 on 8 February (Stacey p. 471); the end of `CR0868` (4th Armoured Division attached to I Corps) is inferred from the next claim (26 February); the Army headquarters place on 8 March, 2 April and 4 May and the corps headquarters places are representative (confidence 0.3).
- **New relations `CR0858` to `CR0872` (15):** `CR0858` (30th Corps attached to the Army, 18 January to 18:00 on 8 March); `CR0859` (I Canadian Corps assigned to the Army from noon on 15 March); `CR0860` (II Canadian Corps assigned to the Army from 23:59 on 1 April); `CR0861` (II Canadian Corps assigned to Second Army, 20 March to 23:59 on 1 April; start day only); `CR0862` and `CR0864` (3rd British Division attached to II Canadian Corps, 29 December to 19 January and 28 April to 6 May); `CR0863` (43rd Division attached to II Canadian Corps from 21 February, end unknown); `CR0865` (49th Division attached to I Canadian Corps from noon on 15 March, end unknown); `CR0866` and `CR0867` (3rd Canadian Division attached to the 30th Corps, 8 to 15 February and 20 to 28 March noon); `CR0868` (4th Armoured Division attached to I Corps from 26 January); `CR0869` (1st Division attached to II Canadian Corps, 3 April noon to 13 April); `CR0870` (1st Division assigned to I Canadian Corps from 3 April); `CR0871` and `CR0872` (5th Armoured Division assigned to I Canadian Corps from 31 March, to II Canadian Corps from 21 April). 104 evidence links (2,132 in all). Polish, Belgian, Dutch and Czech attachments are not written (British and Polish batch).
- **`CR0845` (I Corps under the Army, assigned): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`. New value: `valid_to` 1945-04-02, time 12:00, basis `narrative`. Reason: Stacey p. 545 (the 1st British Corps returned to General Dempsey command at noon on 2 April). Annotated in the relation note.
- **`CR0847` (II Canadian Corps under the Army, assigned): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`. New value: `valid_to` 1945-03-20, no hour, basis `narrative`. Reason: Stacey p. 531 (the corps passed under the complete operational command of Second Army on 20 March; the hour is not given). Annotated in the relation note; the new relation `CR0860` starts at 23:59 on 1 April (p. 545).
- **`CR0857` (4th Armoured Division, Army Reserve): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`, end unknown. New value: `valid_to` 1945-01-25, basis `inferred_from_next_claim`, end unknown, the end lying between 23 December 1944 and 25 January 1945 (the division was under I Corps again from 26 January). Reason: the next claim of the division, `C1389` of 26 January 1945 (Operation Elephant, Stacey pp. 452-453), places it under I Corps and not in Army Reserve; the day Army Reserve ended is not stated. Annotated in the relation note.
- **Overlap rows:** three rows in `data/command_relation_overlaps.csv`, kind `transfer_dates_differ`, for same-day handovers: `CR0847` and `CR0861` (20 March, the source gives the day only), `CR0860` and `CR0861` (1 April, 23:59), `CR0871` and `CR0872` (21 April, the day only). The explanation of each says what the source gives. Under the day-level rule both relations cover the boundary day.
- **Left open on purpose:** `CR0850` and `CR0851` (7th Armoured and Guards Armoured Divisions attached to II Canadian Corps from 20 July 1944): Stacey gives no dated end (p. 283 is only an order to move the 7th Armoured Division to Second Army at once). Flagged for the British batch.
- **Places:** 28 new, `PL0694` to `PL0721` (geocoded with Nominatim, place centres; precision 3 to 10 km): Kapelsche Veer, Uden, Reichswald, Wyler, Calcar, Udem, Hochwald, Xanten, Millingen, Emmerich, Zutphen, Coevorden, Apeldoorn, Groningen, Harskamp, Wageningen, Leer, Aurich, Bad Zwischenahn, Moyland, Didam, Delfzijl, Varel, Steenwijk, Netterden, Almen, Barneveld, Grossenkneten. Existing places reused: Nijmegen, Tilburg, Arnhem, Kleve, Rees, Oldenburg, Utrecht, Antwerp, Boxtel.

### Item 5: tests

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`**: scenario 15 added (22 expectations: the noon handovers of I Corps (2 April) and I Canadian Corps (15 March), II Canadian Corps under the Army, Second Army and the Army again (day only on 20 March, 23:59 on 1 April), the 30th Corps attachment of 8 March, the 3rd Division with the 30th Corps in February and March, the 4th Armoured Division Reserve and I Corps attachment, the 1st and 5th Divisions, the 49th Division, `C1343`); 243 expectations in all.

### Item 6: spot-check corrections

- A page-reading spot-check of all 65 claims against the printed pages of Stacey found 7 weak claims, corrected before the push: `C1348` (the place where the 1st Corps stood is not stated, wording changed to the explanation given to General Foulkes), `C1357` (an unsupported statement about the reduction of Deventer removed), `C1363` (the act said the 49th Division captured Arnhem, Stacey says its assault succeeded quickly), `C1364` (an unsupported date relation to the 5th Armoured Division move removed), `C1381` (the attack on Udem is described as in Stacey: 21:00 start, sniping only by 04:00, the North Nova Scotia Highlanders pushing into the centre afterwards), `C1390` (evacuation hedged with it appears, as Stacey says), `C1407` (the 11th Brigade is not named on the pages read and was removed). All 151 quotations in the new notes were checked by script (0 missing).

### Item 7: documents

- **Counts:** 65 claims (`C1343` to `C1407`), 15 relations, 104 evidence links, 3 overlaps (30 in all), 3 units activated, 28 places, 3 operations (60 in all). Next free ids: `C1408`, `CR0873`, `PL0722`, `R669`, `G028`.
- **Checks:** `validate` OK (260 claim files, 1,407 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups; 11 warnings: the 8 of Canadian Batch 1 plus `CAN-INF-001D` line 4 (13 April), `CAN-INF-003D` lines 26 and 29 (15 February and 28 March): each claim that ends an attachment is listed under the new operational parent). The who_controls grid (10,620 questions) changed in 380 answers and added 120 new ones (the three new units); changes are the carry-forward answers of the Canadian units and the British divisions of the Army becoming certain, and the new attachments.
- **Documents:** README counts and a Canadian Batch 2 sentence; `docs/HANDOFF.md` (state table, validator and test baselines, decision log); `docs/roadmap.md` (table row and section); `docs/sources.md` (scope of Stacey); `docs/coverage.md` and `build/` regenerated; `docs/review/` sample regenerated.

## canadian-batch1-jun-dec-1944: Canadian Batch 1, July to December 1944 (28 October 2026)

Branch `canadian-batch1-jun-dec-1944`, cut from `main` (`259f572`, 1,277 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups, 201 semantic expectations); compare it with `main` (`https://github.com/scottstarsman/UnitLocationData/compare/main...canadian-batch1-jun-dec-1944`). Task approved by Scott on 28 October 2026 ("Let's leave Italy out. We'll do that as a separate theater. Please proceed with the 1944 Canadians"). Scope: First Canadian Army, II Canadian Corps and the 2nd, 3rd and 4th Canadian Divisions (infantry and armoured) from 4 July to 31 December 1944. Not covered: I Canadian Corps, the 5th Canadian Armoured Division, the 1st Canadian Division, independent and armoured brigades, Italy. Attached British, Polish and US formations appear only as command relations.

### Item 1: reference and operations

- **Reference `R668`**: Stacey, The Victory Campaign (Official History of the Canadian Army, Vol. III, canada.ca PDF), source type `official_history`, family `canada_ca`, accessed and checked 28 October 2026. Printed page numbers are cited.
- **Six operations** appended to `data/operations.csv` (not re-sorted): `OP-ATLANTIC` (18 to 21 July, parent `OP-CAMP-NORMANDY`), `OP-SPRING` (25 July, `OP-CAMP-NORTHERN-FRANCE`), `OP-TOTALIZE` (7 to 10 August, `OP-CAMP-NORTHERN-FRANCE`), `OP-WELLHIT` (17 to 22 September, `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`), `OP-UNDERGO` (25 September to 1 October, `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`), `OP-SWITCHBACK` (6 October to 3 November, a phase of `OP-SCHELDT`). Three rows in `data/operation_relations.csv`: Atlantic concurrent with Goodwood, Spring follows Atlantic, Totalize follows Spring. No `OP-TRACTABLE`: the Tractable claims use `OP-FALAISE`.
- **`schema/vocabularies.json`** (`operation_date_basis`, `official_history`): old text "Dates from an official history: a US Army Green Book or CMH publication."; new text adds "or a national official history such as the Canadian Army official history (Stacey, The Victory Campaign)". Reason: the six new operations take their dates from Stacey.

### Item 2: First Canadian Army, II Canadian Corps and I Corps

- **Claims:** `C1279` to `C1285` (Army, 7), `C1286` to `C1295` (II Canadian Corps, 10), and `C1278` (I Corps, 23 July: a minimal command-change row that exists only so that a higher-unit claim ends `CR0067` and starts `CR0845`). New claim files `CAN-ARMY-001A.csv`, `CAN-COR-002C.csv`; one row appended to `UK-COR-001C.csv`. Units `CAN-ARMY-001A` and `CAN-COR-002C` became active.
- **Relations:** `CR0844` (Army to 21st Army Group, 23 July 12:00), `CR0845` (I Corps to the Army, 23 July 12:00), `CR0846` (II Canadian Corps to Second Army, 11 July 15:00 to 31 July 12:00), `CR0847` (II Canadian Corps to the Army, 31 July 12:00), `CR0848` (2nd Division to the Corps, 11 July 15:00), `CR0849` (3rd Division to the Corps, 11 July 15:00), `CR0850` and `CR0851` (7th Armoured and Guards Armoured Divisions attached from 20 July, end unknown), `CR0852` (51st Highland Division attached, 4 August 00:00 to 9 August 13:00), `CR0853` (1st Polish Armoured Division attached, 5 August 06:00 to 28 September).
- **`CR0067` (I Corps under Second Army, assigned): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`. New value: `valid_to` 1944-07-23, time 12:00, basis `narrative`. Reason: Stacey p. 196 (the front held by the corps passed to First Canadian Army at noon on 23 July) and p. 198 (the corps served under the Army). Annotated in the relation note.

### Item 3: 3rd Canadian Infantry Division

- **Claims:** `C1296` to `C1315` (20 rows, 4 July to 13 November; Carpiquet, Caen, Colombelles, Tilly-la-Campagne, Rouvres, Point 168, Trun, Rouen, Boulogne, Calais, the Leopold Canal, Hoofdplaat, Breskens, Nijmegen). Commanders from Appendix G: Keller to 8 August, Blackader acting 8 to 18 August, Spry from 18 August.
- **`CR0003` (3rd Division under I Corps, assigned): closed.** Old value: `valid_to` empty, basis `open_at_last_claim`. New value: `valid_to` 1944-07-11, time 15:00, basis `narrative`. Reason: Stacey p. 166 (II Canadian Corps took over the Caen front at 15:00 on 11 July with the 3rd Division under command); the division passes to `CR0849`. Annotated in the relation note.
- **Overlap rows:** three rows in `data/command_relation_overlaps.csv`, kind `transfer_dates_differ`, for same-day handovers at a recorded hour: `CR0003` and `CR0849` (11 July), `CR0067` and `CR0845` (23 July), `CR0846` and `CR0847` (31 July). Under the day-level rule both relations cover the boundary day; the sources give the hour and do not disagree.

### Item 4: 2nd Canadian Infantry Division

- **Claims:** `C1316` to `C1331` (16 rows, 7 July to 7 December; Juno area, Louvigny, Verrieres, Bretteville-sur-Laize, Falaise, the Foret de la Londe, Dieppe, Antwerp, Woensdrecht, Sloedam, Groesbeek). Commanders: Foulkes to 26 September, Keefler acting from 27 September, Matthews from 10 November.
- **Relation `CR0854`** (2nd Division attached to I Corps, 26 September to midnight 6-7 October, stated start and end). The standing relation to II Canadian Corps is not closed.

### Item 5: 4th Canadian Armoured Division

- **Claims:** `C1332` to `C1342` (11 rows, 31 July to 23 December; Bourguebus, Cintheaux, Olendon, Damblainville, Trun (confidence 0.9: Stacey p. 256 and CMH Breakout and Pursuit p. 532), St. Lambert, Criquebeuf, Antwerp, Esschen, Steenbergen, Boxtel). Commanders: Kitching to 21 August, Foster 22 August to 30 November, Vokes from 1 December.
- **Relations:** `CR0855` (to II Canadian Corps, assigned, from 31 July, start inferred), `CR0856` (attached to I Corps from 18 October 00:00; end inferred from the next claim, 22 December), `CR0857` (Army Reserve, from 23 December, start is the claim date).

### Places, tests and documents

- **Places:** 29 new, `PL0665` to `PL0693` (geocoded with Nominatim, place centres; precision 3 to 10 km). Existing places reused: Caen, the Caen sector, Juno, Nijmegen, Antwerp, Breskens, Tilburg (not used), Bretteville-sur-Laize, St. Lambert, Walcheren.
- **Counts:** 65 claims (`C1278` to `C1342`; confidence 0.9 for 1, 0.7 for 32, 0.5 for 26, 0.3 for 6), 14 relations, 83 evidence links (2,028 in all), 3 overlaps (27), 4 units activated (257 active, 110 placeholders). Next free ids: `C1343`, `CR0858`, `PL0694`, `R669`, `G028`.
- **Checks:** `validate` OK (257 claim files, 1,342 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups; 8 warnings: the 7 older ones plus `CAN-INF-002D` line 13, the 7 October claim that ends the I Corps attachment is listed under its operational parent, II Canadian Corps). All 148 quotations in the new notes were checked by script against the Stacey page text and the CMH page. The who_controls grid of 5,020 questions before: 302 answers changed, all for units whose chain passes through I Corps, II Canadian Corps or the Army (CAN-ARM-002B, CAN-INF-003D, UK-COR-001C, UK-ABN-006D, UK-INF-003D, UK-INF-051D, UK-ARM-007D, UK-ARM-GRDD, US-ABN-101D, US-INF-104D); 200 new questions for the new units.
- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`**: scenario 14 added (20 expectations: the hour-level handovers of 11, 23 and 31 July, the two temporary attachments to I Corps, the Army Reserve relation, `C1278`); 221 expectations in all.
- **Documents:** README counts; `docs/HANDOFF.md` (state table, validator and test baselines, decision log); `docs/roadmap.md` (table row and section); `docs/sources.md` (Stacey); `docs/coverage.md` and `build/` regenerated; `docs/review/` sample regenerated.

## cleanup-open-items-oct28: clean-up of open items (28 October 2026)

Branch `cleanup-open-items-oct28`, cut from `main` (`af8ae06`, 1,277 claims, 367 units, 24 conflict groups, 194 semantic expectations); compare it with `main` (`https://github.com/scottstarsman/UnitLocationData/compare/main...cleanup-open-items-oct28`). Task approved by Scott on 28 October 2026 (clean-up task 1: the open conflict groups `G021` to `G025`, the 243rd Division, the parent of the 353rd Division, the stray file `WorldWar2/before.txt`). Every change below lists old value, new value and reason; the claims were not deleted and no claim was added.

### Item 1: the open conflict groups `G021` to `G025`

Read: HANDOFF and roadmap entries, the claim rows of each group, and the sources again (Lexikon der Wehrmacht pages of the divisions, corps and Army Group B, the Nafziger file 944BGAA, Breakout and Pursuit ch. 7, Riviera to the Rhine ch. 4, Wikipedia). Rule used: a group is settled only where the sources clearly decide it; a status change is recorded with old value, new value and reason in the notes of every row concerned; no claim was deleted and no date, place, confidence or flag of any of the ten rows changed except where stated.

- **`G022` (Panzer Lehr under the LXXXIV Corps): settled for July.** `C0792` (Lexikon dated list of 15 July): `claim_status` disputed -> preferred. `C0790` (Lexikon table, June): disputed -> alternate. `C0791` (Feldgrau table, July): disputed -> alternate. Reason: the dated lists of the LXXXIV Corps on the Lexikon page of 15 and 21 June do not name the division; Breakout and Pursuit ch. 7 p. 128 has the division in the corps sector by 12 and 13 July; the existing relations keep it under the I SS Panzer Corps to 10 July and under the Seventh Army from 11 July. The day of the subordination is still not dated (between 10 and 15 July). `C0792` also cites `R591` now (source and locator extended).
- **`G024` (716th Division): settled for southern France.** `C0815` (Lexikon text: coastal sector at the Spanish border by 10 July, headquarters Thuir): disputed -> preferred. `C0816` (Lexikon table, July: Normandie): disputed -> alternate. Reason: Riviera to the Rhine ch. 4 p. 66 has the 198th and 716th Divisions under the IV Luftwaffe Field Corps between the Rhone delta and the Spanish border at the landing of 15 August (new reference `R667`), the August row of the Lexikon table also names that corps, and the English Wikipedia article (`R088`, not independent of the Lexikon) gives the removal from the line on 10 July. Date and confidence (0.5) of `C0815` unchanged.
- **`G021` (Army Group B, Kluge): stays open, preferred row unchanged.** `C0714` (17 July, CMH and Wikipedia) preferred, `C0767` (19 July, Schramm) alternate. New evidence: the Lexikon commander list (`R185`, added to both rows) gives 19 July for Kluge and ends Rommel on 17 July, so each date now rests on two families and the earlier reason for the preference (two against one) no longer holds. Kept: the CMH text states the takeover itself, the Lexikon a tenure date; the dates may be two event definitions (in fact from 17 July, listed from 19 July), which no source says. Review sentences on both rows; no value changed.
- **`G023` (12th SS Panzer Division in July): stays open, both rows disputed.** Nafziger (corps reserve of the I SS Panzer Corps) against the Lexikon table (LXXXVI Corps). The Lexikon text of the division (refit north of Falaise from 11 July; Kampfgruppen to the 272nd Division on 17 July and the 21st Panzer Division on 18 July) and the LXXXVI Corps page (Goodwood on 18 July) suggest that the two rows may describe different weeks, but no source names a corps for those days. Review sentences on both rows (`R252` added to `C0783`); no value changed.
- **`G025` (711th Division in July): stays open, both rows disputed; one wrong statement corrected.** `C0817` (Lexikon table: LXXXI Corps) against `C0818` (Nafziger: part of the division under the LXXXVI Corps). Old value: the Event date sentence of `C0817` said that the same table has an earlier July row "z. Vfg." under the Fifteenth Army; new value: the sentence is removed; reason: that row is in the 1941 table of the page, the 1944 table has one July row. New evidence: the Lexikon dated lists of the LXXXI Corps (15 June, `R247`) and the LXXXVI Corps (21 June, `R252`) both name the division, and the English Wikipedia article (`R087`) has its 731st Regiment at Troarn for Goodwood, so the rows may describe different parts of one division; no source says which corps held the whole division in July.
- **Reference:** `R667` added (Riviera to the Rhine ch. 4). No new claim, relation, place or conflict-group id; next free ids `C1278`, `CR0844`, `PL0665`, `R668`, `G028`.
