## review-packet (26 October 2026)

An independent-review packet for an outside reviewer who has read access to the repository and nothing else. No claim, table, schema or validator rule changed; the three commits add documents and one read-only script.

- **`scripts/review_sample.py`** (standard library only) draws a seeded, stratified sample and computes the counts quoted in the brief. Seed 20261026. Claims: 40, with a quota per nationality proportional to the square root of its claims (Canada 2 of 4, Germany 9 of 75, UK 8 of 60, US 21 of 427), then coverage-first inside each nationality over echelon group, event type, confidence and claim status (all 18 event types, all four confidence levels, all three statuses and all five echelon groups are in the sample). Relations: 20 of 375, coverage-first over relationship, start and end basis, status, the two flags, subject nationality, overlap membership and whether a higher unit is named. Ranks are SHA-256 hashes of the seed and the row id, so the draw does not depend on the Python version. `--check` fails when the sample or the counts block of the brief is stale; `--seed`, `--claims` and `--relations` draw another sample.
- **`docs/review/`**: `README.md` (brief: scope, table map, rules, counts, known limitations, how to run the checks, what is out of scope), `checklist.md` (ten areas and how to check a sampled row), `sample.md`, `sample_claims.csv`, `sample_relations.csv` (each sampled row with its cited sources, links and locators, and empty verdict columns), `questions.md` (ten design questions), `findings_template.md` (the format of a finding and how a finding is handled: verify against the sources, accept, decline, defer, record) and `responses.md` (empty until a review comes back).
- **README.** The unit count in the status line is 356, as in the table (it said 354); the layout and the documents list mention the packet.
- **Checks.** Validator: 0 errors and the same three warnings; `build.py --check`, `build_references.py --check`, `check_coordinates.py` and both audits unchanged; `review_sample.py --check` passes. Generated files in `build/` are unchanged.

## integrity-rules (26 October 2026)

Three data integrity rules, one commit each: the claim key (this section's first item), claim-id allocation, and the controlled set of note labels. Validator warnings stay the same three; no claim was changed.

### Rule 1: unit, date and event type identify one claim

- **Rule.** `(unit_id, date, event_type)` is unique across the claims (`uniqueTogether` in `schema/claims.schema.json`, enforced by `scripts/validate.py`). A repeat is allowed only inside one conflict group, or when the pair is listed with a reason in the new hand-maintained table `data/claim_key_exceptions.csv` (`claim_id, other_claim_id, reason`: several actions of one kind by one unit on one day). An exception that is not needed is an error, so the list cannot go stale. The error names both claims and the three ways out. Described in `schema/README.md` ("Claim key") and `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Claim identity", checklist).
- **Audit before the rule.** 566 claims, 566 distinct keys: **0 violations**, so no claim was deleted, merged or edited and the exception table is empty. Looked at for context: 7 pairs of claims share a unit and a date but differ in event type (C0015/C0016, C0199/C0200, C0284/C0483, C0484/C0485, C0333/C0496, C0217/C0481, C0220/C0482: a landing or attack beside a headquarters move, and so on); they are different events and stay as they are. The 14 conflict groups all differ in date or event type (sources disagree about the date), so none needed the group exemption either.
- **Table.** `claim_key_exceptions` is declared in `schema/relations.schema.json` (primary key `claim_id, other_claim_id`, both foreign keys to `claims.claim_id`).

### Rule 2: claim ids

- **State first.** 566 claims, C0001 to C0566, every id once, no gap; every claim id that appears in any of the 38 revisions of the working history is still a claim (no retired id exists). So the new table starts empty.
- **Rule** (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, "Claim ids"): format `C` + at least four digits; next id = highest id in use (claims and retired) + 1; an id that was on `main` stays with its claim, is never reused and never renumbered (a branch renumbers only its own unmerged new ids if two branches took the same number); gaps are allowed (an id that was never used); a claim that has to leave the data is retired in the new hand-maintained table `data/retired_claims.csv` (`claim_id, replaced_by_claim_id, reason`) in the same change; a merely wrong claim is corrected or contradicted, not retired.
- **Validator.** Ids are unique (as before); `data/retired_claims.csv` is checked (header, sorted, no repeated id, a retired id is not a claim, the replacement is a claim, the reason is not blank); a gap that is not retired is printed as a NOTE (none today). Declared in `schema/relations.schema.json` (`retired_claims`, foreign key `replaced_by_claim_id`).
- **History check.** `scripts/check_claim_ids.py [--base REV]` (default `origin/main`) compares with an earlier revision and fails when a claim id of that revision is gone without a retired row, now belongs to another unit, or when a retired id was forgotten or reused. A single checkout cannot show this and CI checks out one commit, so it is run by hand before a pull request (listed in the checklist).

### Rule 3: the note labels are a controlled set

- **Set found.** Sentence-initial `Label: ` phrases of label shape (a capital letter, then at most two lower-case words) in the `notes` of the 566 claims: 19 labels, 632 uses in 118 claims: Event date 108, Event definition 106, Relationship 102 (two with the qualifier `(sequence)`), Location 100, Event support 70, Location support 70, Coordinates 55, Objective check 8 (five with `(Batch 2 review)`), Location caveat 3, and one each of Location approximate, Location support fallback, Coordinate check, Command support, Opponents, Commander, Context, Later, Search, Review. It is the set that `scripts/migrate_claim_references.py` already read, plus `Review` (C0484). Lead-ins that name a source, a batch or an id (`CMH ETO-OB chronology:` 17, `Wikipedia:` 15, `Batch 2 review:` 10, `Breakout-NN pNNN:` and others) are prose, not labels, and stay unchecked; no note was changed.
- **Vocabulary and check.** New vocabulary `note_label` in `schema/vocabularies.json` (value = definition and the frozen count). `scripts/validate.py` (`check_note_labels`) rejects a sentence-initial phrase of label shape that is not in the vocabulary (`unknown label`), and a known label with the wrong case or spelling; a qualifier in parentheses is free. `Wikipedia` (a source name of label shape) is exempted by name. Zero errors on the current data.
- **Process.** Adding a label is one commit: the vocabulary entry with its definition, the table in `schema/README.md` ("Note labels", which also describes the rule and the process), a line here, and at least one row that uses it (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, rule 9).

## place-merges (26 October 2026)

Item 8 of the review in [unit_references_review.md](unit_references_review.md): the five place merges of [places_review.md](places_review.md) (section 7). One commit per merge, after one commit that adds the mechanism. Applied with `scripts/migrate_place_merges.py` (`--merge 1` to `5`; every edit asserts the old value) and checked with `scripts/verify_place_merges.py`.

- **New table `data/place_merges.csv`** (`retired_place_id, merged_into_place_id, retired_name, note`): a place id is never reused, so a merged-away id leaves `data/places.csv` and is recorded here with the survivor and the reason. `scripts/places.py` has `load_merges()` and `resolve()`. It is declared in `schema/relations.schema.json` (primary key, foreign key to `places.place_id`) and described in `schema/README.md` and `docs/methodology.md` ("Merging two places later").
- **Validator.** The table is checked (header, sorted, no blank or repeated value, the retired id is not a place, the survivor is a live place); a claim whose `place_id` is a retired id is an error that names the survivor; an alias may not be another place's alias either (before: only another place's name). The warnings are the same three.
- **The five merges** (survivor first): PL0071 Caumont-l'Éventé absorbs PL0070 Caumont; PL0209 Mantes-la-Jolie, Yvelines absorbs PL0208 Mantes-Gassicourt, Yvelines (Gassicourt is a district of Mantes-la-Jolie, annexed in 1930, checked against Wikipedia first; the town was called Mantes-Gassicourt until 1953); PL0045 Brest, Finistère absorbs PL0294 Recouvrance, Brest; PL0258 Omaha Beach absorbs PL0259 Omaha Beach sector; PL0173 Hill 112 absorbs PL0174 Hill 112 / Odon salient. Places 383 -> 378 (288 point, 79 area, 16 line became 285 point, 77 area, 16 line).
- **Claims.** Six claims moved (C0104, C0330, C0451, C0408, C0006, C0196): each keeps its own `precision_km`, point and `coord_override`; the retired place's context is in `location_qualifier` (C0330 and C0451 `Mantes-Gassicourt, Seine crossing`, C0408 `Recouvrance district`, C0196 `Odon salient`; C0104 and C0006 keep theirs). Aliases added: `Caumont`, `Mantes-Gassicourt`, `Recouvrance`. All five pairs had the same `coord_reference_id`, so provenance did not change.
- **What changed in the outputs.** `build/all_claims.csv`: only place_id, place_name, location_name and location_qualifier (and the place columns that follow the new place) of those six claims. `build/claims.geojson`: no position changed. `who_controls` answers on the 48-query sample are the same. `build/places.csv`, `build/places.geojson` and `docs/coverage.md`: five places fewer, the alias and claim counts of the survivors.
- **Not changed.** The 33 other groups of co-located places in `docs/places_review.md` stay as they were (38 groups became 35: the groups at Brest, Hill 112 and Omaha Beach no longer exist, those at Caumont and Mantes-la-Jolie lost one place each). Claim C0006 (the 352nd Division headquarters at Le Molay-Littry, inland) now sits on the Omaha Beach point as before and is flagged there for its own place later.

## review-items-1-7 (26 October 2026)

Items 1 to 7 of the review in [unit_references_review.md](unit_references_review.md) (the decisions listed there; item 8, the five place merges, is not part of this branch and stays open). One commit per item. Applied with `scripts/migrate_review_items.py` (run once per item; every edit asserts the old value).

### Item 1: the six former `attached:` entries

- **GER-FJR-006 with the 91st Luftlande Division (C0010) is now a relation, `CR0372`** (`attached`, from 6 June 1944, `end_unknown`). The source is the CMH volume Cross-Channel Attack (Harrison): ch. VII p. 237-238 says the 91st Division went into combat with an attached parachute regiment and p. 259-260 that Seventh Army ordered the division to take over command of the 6th Parachute Regiment on arrival; ch. VIII p. 292-293 has the regiment "under control of the 91st Division" on 6 June, and note 47 says the subordination ordered by Seventh Army was apparently not effected (von der Heydte directed the regiment without orders from the division). It is recorded as ordered, with that caveat in the relation's note; the start is the claim date because no source dates the order. References R447 and R448 (already in the table) are added to C0010 (`reference_ids`, `source`, `source_locator`, role `command_relationship` in `data/claim_reference_roles.csv`).
- **New evidence role `listed_by_higher_unit`** (vocabulary `relation_claim_role`, `data/command_relation_claims.csv`). A relation is evidenced by claims of its subject unit; the 6th Fallschirmjäger Regiment has no claim of its own, so the claim of the higher unit that lists it (C0010) is the evidence. Such a link is evidence only: it is not one of the listing claim's own relationships (the claim's rebuilt `command_relationships` does not change), the subject must be listed in the claim's `composing_units`, the link's `stated_note` keeps what the claim said, and a claim that lists a relation before it starts must state that start. `scripts/command_relations.py` and `scripts/validate.py` know the role.
- **The other five are not units of the table** (the 56th Infantry Brigade C0017, the 1st Canadian Parachute Battalion C0018, the 116th Infantry Regiment C0025, the 2nd and 5th Ranger Battalions C0031, the 2nd Irish Guards C0217; scope is division and above), so no unit and no relation was added. The word `(attached)` is removed from their `composing_text` entries and each relationship is kept, with the claim's date and source as stated, in the new [deferred_relations.md](deferred_relations.md) (rows D01 to D06). `scripts/validate.py` checks that file (claim exists and belongs to the claim unit, higher unit is a unit, relationship is in the vocabulary, the entry is still in the claim's `composing_text`).

### Item 2: the word "attached" is prohibited in the composition and engagement text

- **Rule.** `unit_refs.check` (run by `validate.py`) rejects the whole word `attach`, `attached`, `attaches`, `attaching`, `attachment` or `attachments` (any case, also after a hyphen) in `composing_text` (every entry and every qualifier in parentheses) and in `engaged_note`. The id columns carry no words. `detached` is a different word and is not caught. The former `attached:` prefix rule is covered by it.
- **17 claims converted** (the 12 plain entries of the review plus the 5 embedded ones; the 8 former `attached:` entries were done before and in item 1). Each claim's source was checked first. Recorded in `data/command_relations.csv` or as extra evidence of an existing relation (the claim of the higher unit, role `listed_by_higher_unit`, the claim's earlier wording kept in `stated_note`): C0105 (new relation, the 30th Division attached to XIX Corps from 13 June, CMH ch. IX p. 376-377, closed on 14 June by the division's next claim), C0089 (new relation, Kampfgruppe Heintz attached to the 17th SS Panzergrenadier Division, CMH ch. IX p. 378-379, start = claim date), and C0120 (CR0100), C0490 (CR0277), C0161 (CR0032), C0493 (CR0177), C0127 (CR0020), C0170 (CR0023), C0173 (CR0030) as additional evidence of relations that already existed (C0100, Combat Command A with the 101st Airborne Division, belongs to the 2nd Armored Division and not to the higher unit of CR0175, so it only loses the word). Not a unit of the table, moved to `docs/deferred_relations.md` (D07 to D11): C0506 (Combat Command B, 3rd Armored Division), C0507 (22nd Infantry Regiment), C0493 (the 22nd Infantry with Combat Command A), C0164 (C Squadron, 2nd County of London Yeomanry), C0211 (2nd Battalion, 915th Regiment, to the 916th Regiment, CMH ch. VIII). Relationship wording removed with no relation: C0037 (`GER-PZ-LEHRD (planned)`: the Panzer Lehr Division was only planned to join, CMH), C0030 (VII Corps with the 82nd and 101st Airborne Divisions; both divisions already have their relation with VII Corps), C0094 (4th Armoured Brigade, now `UK-ARM-004B (for Operation Epsom)`: the claim dates 13 June, the relation CR0059 starts 26 June, so the claim is not linked). C0094, C0161 and C0493 also gain an id in `composing_units` (UK-ARM-004B, GER-KG-WEIDINGER, US-CCA-002D); the five embedded ones get theirs in item 3.
- **Nothing else changed.** `who_controls` answers are the same on the 48-query sample; only the list of supporting claims of two lines grew (GER-KG-FREY gains C0170, US-CCA-002D gains C0493). The validator warnings are the same three.
- **Other words.** `notes` (140 claims `assign*`, 19 `operational control`/OPCON, 71 `attach*`, 7 placed/came under, 2 `subordinat*`, 1 under command), `current_activity`, `location_qualifier` and `strength_json` keep the word `attached` where it is the source's own description of a fact; they are free narrative and not parsed. Not banned: `composing_text` and `engaged_note` contain one `assigned` (C0052 `GER-SSPG-017D (assigned for blocking mission near Carentan)`, a task and not a command relation) and four `reinforced`. Recommendation: keep the ban to `composing_text` and `engaged_note` (the fields that were turned into ids), do not ban the narrative columns; the relationship words that matter are in the relations table.

### Item 3: the ten embedded unit ids

- **Ten entries in nine claims** (C0006, C0089, C0100, C0126, C0127 with two entries (Meyer and Boehm), C0145, C0170, C0173, C0184) had a unit id inside a free-text sentence ("battle group from the 1st SS Division: Kampfgruppe Frey (GER-KG-FREY; from 28 June)"). Each is now `<unit_id> (qualifier)` with the id in `composing_units`, like the other 25 qualified entries; the qualifier keeps the wording of the source minus the name that the id now supplies and minus the word "attached" (item 2). Nine ids are new in `composing_units` (GER-KG-MEYER twice, GER-KG-BOEHM, GER-KG-HEINTZ, GER-KG-ROHRBACH twice, GER-KG-FREY, GER-KG-WEIDINGER, GER-KG-VONLUCK; US-CCA-002D was already listed in C0100).
- **Validator.** An id inside an unqualified free-text entry of `composing_text` is now an error (it had to exist before): write `<unit_id> (qualifier)`.
- The relationships behind these entries are the relations of item 2 and the existing ones (see the evidence links of CR0020, CR0023, CR0030, CR0374).

### Item 4: "First Army" in C0039

- **Checked, left as text.** C0039 (21st Army Group, 7 June 1944) cites two pages about Montgomery's headquarters at Creullet (actu.fr, dday-overlord.com, read on 26 October 2026): neither mentions a First Army or any army (they give the dates of the visits, 7 to 22 June at Creullet and the move to Blay on 23 June). The claim's `composing_text` is `First Army` and the source does not decide which one. The US First Army was under the operational control of the 21st Army Group from 6 June (CR0166) and the First Canadian Army belonged to its order of battle (it was not yet in Normandy), so the name alone does not decide it; the evidence is not conclusive and the entry stays text (no id is guessed). If a source naming the army is found, replace it with the id (probably `US-ARMY-001A`) and add the claim to CR0166.

### Item 5: the 8th Armoured Brigade under the 50th Division (CR0061)

- **Sources checked** (read on 26 October 2026): Wikipedia, Gold Beach (the 50th Division order of battle lists the 8th Armoured Brigade under "Formations attached for assault phase"; the invasion section says the division was "supported by the 8th Armoured Brigade"); Wikipedia, 8th Armoured Brigade (it became an independent brigade in November 1942 and landed on 6 June supporting the 50th Division); British Military History, Independent Armoured Brigades, p. 7 (the brigade "landed in Normandy on 6 June 1944, supporting the 50th Infantry Division" and took part in the battle of "The Oden" (the Odon) from 25 June "under command of XXX Corps"); Pegasus Archive, XXX Corps (the brigade is listed as attached to the corps headquarters, with the 56th Infantry Brigade and elements of the 79th Armoured Division). None supports `organic`.
- **Fix.** CR0061 is `attached` (was `organic`), `end_unknown` true, start 6 June 1944 (the claim date, the landing; no source dates the end), with the three new references R512 (Gold Beach), R513 (British Military History PDF, p. 7) and R514 (Pegasus XXX Corps) as `command_relationship` evidence of C0020, a note stating the sources and the one disagreement (Pegasus puts the attachment at corps level; it is consistent with the corps holding the brigade and attaching it to the division for the assault). The 50th Division's claim C0017, which lists the brigade in `composing_units`, is added as evidence (`listed_by_higher_unit`). The standing parent is not named by a source, so there is an `unknown` placeholder for the brigade (CR0375, the same pattern as the 4th Armoured Brigade, CR0058) and the validator does not gain a warning. C0020's `parent_unit_id` (the 50th Division) is unchanged and its notes carry a review remark.
- **who_controls.** The 48-query sample has no query for this brigade, so its answers are unchanged there. The brigade itself changes: `who_controls.py UK-ARM-008B 1944-06-06` answered "no relationship interval" (organic is not an interval of the derived table) and now answers attached to the 50th Division, with the corps and army chain. The interval is open (no source dates the end; the same behaviour as the other open `attached` relations), so a later date such as 1 September 1944 gets the same answer: this follows the data, and a dated end would need a source.

### Item 6: echelon vocabulary and the id letter convention

- **`battalion` added** to the `echelon` vocabulary (`schema/vocabularies.json`, `schema/README.md`); its rank (1) was already in `echelon_rank`, and it is in `display_order` between `regiment/battle_group` and `other` (so `docs/coverage.md` has a `battalion` row). **No separate `regiment` value**: the existing `regiment/battle_group` covers a regiment and a regiment-sized battle group (minimal change; a split can be made later if a regiment needs different handling).
- **Id letter convention documented** (`schema/README.md` "unit_id pattern", `docs/methodology.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`) and written as data in the new vocabulary `echelon_id_letter` (G army group, A army, C corps, D division, B brigade, H other HQ; `no_letter`: `regiment/battle_group`, `battalion`; the combat-command exception `US-CCA-002D`, `US-CCR-004D`, whose final D is the parent division).
- **The two placeholders.** `GER-SSHPZ-101` (SS Heavy Panzer Battalion 101): echelon `other` to `battalion`. `GER-FJR-006` (6th Fallschirmjäger Regiment): stays `regiment/battle_group`. **Their ids are not changed**: the convention gives regiments, battle groups and battalions no letter, so both existing ids already follow it (no alias or old-id note is needed; nothing refers to a new id). Their `unit_note` says so.
- **Validator.** (a) `battalion` is accepted as an echelon value through the vocabulary; (b) new check on `data/units.csv`: an id that ends in a digit and one of G, A, C, D, B, H must have the matching echelon, with the combat-command pattern as the only exception (all 356 current units pass; ids without a letter, including ten division placeholders and `GER-AA-LUETTW`, are accepted as before and are not renamed).
- **Not changed.** Claims have no `battalion` value in use; `who_controls` and the audits are unchanged.

### Item 7: the regenerated-text differences are accepted

- **Accepted, no data change.** The 24 cells in which the `engaged_with` and `composing_units` text regenerated from the ids differs from the old text (the display name replacing a short or variant form such as "Combat Command A", "17th SS Panzergrenadier Division", "Nineteenth Army", "3rd Canadian Infantry Division"; the removed `attached:` prefix; two cells that list the same entries in another order) are accepted as the new wording. Recorded in `docs/unit_references_review.md` (item 7).
- **Losslessness after items 1 to 7** (`scripts/verify_review_items.py --base <commit before the branch>`, one-off, kept as the record). 38 cells in 27 of the 566 claims differ in `composing_units` (11, all an id added) or `composing_text` (27, the relationship wording removed or the entry converted); `engaged_with` and `engaged_note` are identical in all 566. Every changed claim has a stated reason in the script (item, and whether the relationship is now a relation, evidence of an existing relation or a row of `docs/deferred_relations.md`), and the script fails if a word of the old derived `composing_units_names` is missing from the new one other than `attached`, a raw unit id now carried by `composing_units` or a preposition of the removed wording.
- **Status of the review file.** Items 1 to 7 are marked done in `docs/unit_references_review.md`; item 8 (the five place merges, `docs/places_review.md`) is not part of this branch and stays open.

## unit-ids-in-text-columns (27 October 2026)

Recommendation 3 (F3, unit references in text columns, the claim part) of [normalization_review.md](normalization_review.md). Applied with `scripts/migrate_unit_ids.py` (run once; `--verify HEAD` before the commit regenerates the old strings from the new data and diffs them). What was mapped and what is left for a decision: [unit_references_review.md](unit_references_review.md).

- **`engaged_with` is a `;` list of unit ids.** 157 claims have a value: 22 are `unknown` (unchanged, the only sentinel) and 135 name 177 opponents (58 distinct strings; the review's 60 counted two halves of one entry split at a `;` inside parentheses). 174 of 177 resolve to a unit id: 161 by display name, 8 by an alias (`Canadian 3rd Infantry Division`, `German 17th SS Panzergrenadier Division`, `German Nineteenth Army`) and 5 to the two new placeholders. 3 generic opponents ('British Royal Air Force', 'German Cherbourg naval forces (Admiral Hennecke)', 'British forces (Gold Beach sector; unit not named by CMH)') are in the new column `engaged_note`, verbatim; no id was invented for them.
- **List column, not a join table.** There is nothing to store per link beyond (claim, opponent), so `claim_engagements` would only repeat the claim file; a `;` list of ids matches `reference_ids` and keeps the claim files readable. The foreign key is declared in `claims.schema.json` (`separator`, `alsoAllowed` `unknown`) like `reference_ids`. Command relations stay a table because they have intervals and evidence.
- **Two placeholder units added** (`data/units.csv`, 354 to 356 rows): `GER-FJR-006` 6th Fallschirmjäger Regiment (regiment/battle_group, Luftwaffe; opponent in C0072, C0085, C0088) and `GER-SSHPZ-101` SS Heavy Panzer Battalion 101 (echelon `other`, Waffen-SS; opponent in C0096, C0097). Nationality Germany, theater western_europe, no parent, no formed date, a unit note saying why they exist.
- **`composing_units` is a `;` list of unit ids, `composing_text` is the new column for the rest.** 101 claims with a list (8 more are `unknown`, unchanged), 318 entries: 116 became ids (91 plain, 25 with a qualifier such as `(elements)` or `(from noon 28 July)` kept as `<unit_id> (<qualifier>)` in `composing_text`; 24 of those qualifiers were in the old text, one is the `(attached)` of C0010), 202 stay free text (regiments, battalions, companies and remarks that are not units; 10 embed a unit id; 'First Army' is ambiguous). 69 distinct units are now listed by id.
- **`attached:` forbidden.** Eight entries had the prefix (C0002, C0010, C0017, C0018, C0025, C0031, C0032, C0217). Two (C0002, C0032) are already relations in `data/command_relations.csv` (CR0001, CR0087) and became plain components; the other six (one unit with an id, five not units of the table) keep an `(attached)` qualifier and are listed for a decision. No relation was invented.
- **Validator.** Foreign keys for both id columns; no duplicate, no self, an opponent on the other side (`nationalities.alliance`); the free-text columns must not repeat the name of a unit that has an id, start with `attached:`, or contain an id that does not exist; qualifier entries need their id in `composing_units`; canonical `; ` joins. The old name-matching warning (`engaged_index`) is gone. Warnings 12 to 3: the 9 caused by unresolved opponents cleared (the other 3 are the `GER-COR-084C` list of more than three opponents and two 'attached entry without a standing parent' warnings, unchanged).
- **Scripts and outputs.** New `scripts/unit_refs.py` (list splitting, name resolution, the checks, the derived names); `common.load_all_claims()` derives `engaged_with_names` and `composing_units_names`; `build/all_claims.csv` carries both next to the id columns (54 columns, was 50: the two stored columns and the two derived ones; the old strings are regenerated by the derived columns). `build/coverage.csv` and `docs/coverage.md` count the two placeholders. `who_controls.py` gives identical answers on the 48-query sample. The historical migration scripts carry a NOTE.
- **Regeneration check.** The old `engaged_with` and `composing_units` strings are regenerated from the new data on the 157 and 109 non-blank cells (`unknown` included); 24 cells differ, all explained: variant spellings replaced by the display name (8 engaged: 3rd Canadian Infantry Division, 17th SS Panzergrenadier Division Götz von Berlichingen, Nineteenth Army (Germany)), the removed `attached:` prefix (8), short names replaced by unit names (6), 2 cells in another order. Every other column of the 566 claims and every old unit row is identical.
- **Not changed.** The 5 suggested place merges (still open in [places_review.md](places_review.md)), the branch/echelon split, commanders, `claim_evidence`, the lifecycle flags, the `units.csv` source columns (the third part of F3) and command relations.

## places-table (27 October 2026)

Recommendations 2 (F2, places) and the derived-value part of 6 (F6) of [normalization_review.md](normalization_review.md), on one branch. Applied with `scripts/migrate_places.py` (run once; `--verify HEAD` before the commit re-checks every old value against the new layout). The places that need a decision are in [places_review.md](places_review.md).

- **New table.** `data/places.csv` (383 places, `PL0001`...: 288 point, 79 area, 16 line; 14 aliases on 13 places; country FR 332, BE 28, NL 10, DE 5, GB 4, LU 4) with `schema/places.schema.json`. The 421 old `location_name` values collapsed to 383 places; 26 places absorb 2-5 names each, only where the folded first name part matched and the points were within 3 km. Places that merely share a geocoded point (a town and the sector or front built on it) stay separate: 38 shared-point groups (105 places) are listed for a decision.
- **Claim files.** `location_name` is replaced by `place_id` (foreign key), `location_qualifier` (the `vicinity` marker and the parenthetical claim context) and `coord_override`. Claim `latitude`/`longitude` stay; the validator requires them to equal the place's point, except for 20 claims whose own point differs by 37 m to 1.66 km and carries a reason in `coord_override`. Claim files: 559,083 to 521,464 bytes.
- **Coordinate provenance.** The `; coords: ...` tail is removed from all 566 `source` cells and now belongs to the place (`coord_reference_id` R001/R002, `coord_source_note`; four hand-worked points have a blank reference). The `match_pattern` column of `references_meta.csv` (it matched that tail) is removed.
- **Derived, no longer stored.** `location_temporal_offset_days` (0 of 354 differed), `strength_status` (the destroyed flag, else the new `strength_json.status`, else `unknown`; regenerated values equal the old ones on all 566 rows) and the 561 mechanical `location` roles of the coordinate services (`claim_reference_roles.csv` 619 to 58 rows; `build_references.derive()` rebuilds them, `build/claim_references.csv` unchanged). Of the 18 `strength_status` rows with no `strength_json` figures, 13 stay `unknown`, 2 follow from `destroyed`, and 3 became status-only `strength_json` records quoting their own notes (C0132, C0127 heavily_depleted; C0189 reduced). The three lifecycle flags are kept (not derivable).
- **Validator and checks.** New place checks (unique ids and names, sorted, vocabularies `place_kind` and `country`, aliases, coordinate reference is a geocoder and is cited by a claim at the place, every claim point equals its place's point unless overridden, override within 5 km), derived roles must not be stored, `strength_json.status` vocabulary. The independence count for confidence 0.9 ignores `gazetteer_geocoder` references (0 warnings before and after; 391 claims rest on one non-geocoder family, information only). `check_coordinates.py` checks claim/place consistency, warns for a point in the open Atlantic and, with `--duplicates`, lists points within 1 km.
- **Scripts.** `common.load_all_claims()` derives `location_name`, the offset, `strength_status` and the place columns; `build.py` writes `build/places.csv` (with `claim_count`) and `build/places.geojson` and adds `place_id`, `place_name`, `country`, `region`, `coord_reference_id`, `coord_source_note` to `build/all_claims.csv`. The historical migration scripts carry a NOTE.
- **Derived outputs.** `build/all_claims.csv` differs only in the new columns, `source` (coordinate tail removed, 566), `location_name` (27 rows, spelling normalised among merged names) and `strength_json` (10 rows, `status` added). `build/claims.geojson`: positions identical; properties add `place_id` and change `location_name` on the same 27 rows. `who_controls.py` gives identical answers on the 48-query sample; both audits and the validator warnings (12) are unchanged.
- **Not changed.** `engaged_with` / `composing_units` ids, the branch/echelon split, commanders, `claim_evidence`, and command relations beyond keeping them working.

## command-relations-table (26 October 2026)

Recommendation 1 of [normalization_review.md](normalization_review.md): command relationships become a table. Applied with `scripts/migrate_command_relations.py` (run once; `--verify 54933a8` re-checks the migration against the claim files of the commit before it).

- **New tables.** `data/command_relations.csv` (master: 371 relation rows, `CR0001`...; 348 distinct subject/higher/relationship triples, the rest are further intervals of the same triple, placeholders and entries known only from alternate or disputed claims), `data/command_relation_claims.csv` (evidence: 1,076 links, with `seq`, `role` and the `stated_*` dates each claim gave) and `data/command_relation_overlaps.csv` (the 19 contradictory overlaps, documented). Fields, keys and foreign keys are in `schema/relations.schema.json`; the new vocabularies `relation_start_basis`, `relation_end_basis`, `relation_claim_role` and `relation_overlap_kind` are in `schema/vocabularies.json`.
- **Claim files.** The `command_relationships` column is removed from all 114 claim files (776,126 to 559,083 bytes); `parent_unit_id` stays and is validated against the claim's relations. `common.load_all_claims()` rebuilds the text for the scripts and `build/all_claims.csv` carries it as a derived column in its old position, so the combined file has the same 42 columns. The rebuilt text equals the old one in meaning for all 566 claims (450 identical, 116 differ only in key order inside the entries: the order is now canonical).
- **Absorbed.** 31 claim entries with no named higher unit (the former `unit_id` null entries and the `unknown` sentinels) are 21 explicit `unknown` placeholder relations (blank `higher_unit_id`, note kept). 53 temporary relations (attached 37, operational_control 11, reserve 3, administrative_control 2) whose end no source dates have `end_unknown` true (38 with an end inferred from the next claim, 15 still open at the unit's last claim). The five undated German parent changes of June 1944 (21st Panzer, 12th SS Panzer, Panzer Lehr, I SS Panzer Corps, II SS Panzer Corps) are flagged `change_undated` (10 relation rows); no date was moved or invented.
- **Scripts.** `build.py` derives `build/command_relations.csv` from the master (all nationalities and dates, placeholders included: 356 rows instead of 315; the 315 old rows are identical), `who_controls.py` reads it (identical answers on every query that had one; a placeholder or a change-undated relation is now reported), `validate.py` checks the three tables (header, values, keys, foreign keys, roles against the stated dates, the interval rules, the evidence against the master, every contradictory overlap documented or an error) and validates the rebuilt per-claim entries as before, `audit_temporal_relationships.py` has three checks more (`master_overlap`, `end_unknown`, `change_flagged`). New library `scripts/command_relations.py`. The historical migration scripts that read the column carry a note.
- **Derived outputs.** `build/all_claims.csv`: only `command_relationships` changes (116 rows, key order). `build/command_relations.csv`: new `relation_id`, `end_unknown`, `change_undated` columns, 41 more rows (20 named relations outside the old window or nations, and the 21 placeholders). All other generated files are byte-identical.
- **Not changed.** The places table, `engaged_with` / `composing_units` ids, the branch/echelon split and the derived columns repeated from `units.csv` are untouched (they depend on this step only through `parent_unit_id`).

## normalize-cheap-fixes (26 October 2026)

Follows the review in [normalization_review.md](normalization_review.md) (items F1 small step, and the "cheap fixes" of its recommendation 4). Applied with `scripts/migrate_normalize.py`.

- **Command relationships.** The redundant `name` is dropped from all 1,061 `command_relationships` entries (the name comes from `units.csv`). The 16 entries with `unit_id` null (formations that are not units: "standing parent not identified" 14, "Army not shown in CMH table" 2) keep their wording as `note`; `unit_id` null is now only allowed with relationship `unknown` and a note. The `unknown` sentinel is gone from `parent_unit_id` (15 claims, now blank) and from `command_relationships` (the same 15 claims, now one entry `{"unit_id": null, "relationship": "unknown", "note": "parent not verified"}`).
- **units.csv.** The column `parent_only` (identical to `record_status = placeholder` on 354 of 354 rows) is removed; 16 columns remain.
- **Nationalities.** New `data/nationalities.csv` (10 rows: code, name, adjective, alt_adjectives, alliance, kind, note). Units, claims and operations refer to its `name`; the validator checks the references and that each unit id starts with the nation's code. The 3 `Allied` units keep the explicit multinational value. `operations.nationality_scope` `German` (2 rows) is now `Germany`.
- **Single-source vocabularies.** Echelon rank and display order, relationship groups (standing, active), `higher_than_organic` and `source_type` are new entries of `schema/vocabularies.json`; `validate.py`, `build.py`, `audit_temporal_relationships.py` and `who_controls.py` read them through `scripts/common.py`. `operations.py` and `build_references.py` read their vocabularies from the same file; the `nationality` and `operation_scope` entries were removed (the table replaces them).
- **Foreign keys.** `foreignKeys` in `claims.schema.json` and `units.schema.json`, the new `relations.schema.json` for the side tables, enforced by `validate.py`; `theater` is now one of the repeated unit columns checked on every claim.
- **Derived outputs.** `build/all_claims.csv`: columns `command_relationships` (all 566 rows) and `parent_unit_id` (15 rows) changed. `build/command_relations.csv`: `higher_name` normalised to `<adjective> <unit_name>` in 64 of 315 rows (it was inconsistent between claims, for example "First Army" and "US First Army"); no other column changed. `build/operations.csv`: `nationality_scope` in 2 rows. All other generated files are byte-identical.

### Confidence of audit-unresolved map rows (2 November 2026)

Scott approved on 2 November 2026: the 434 map rows the visual audit could not confirm within 3 km of their unit's symbol are set to confidence 0.3. 216 Allied rows changed from 0.5 to 0.3 (US 138, UK 50, France 20, Canada 7, Poland 1), each annotated with old value 0.5, new value 0.3, reason: visual audit could not confirm symbol position within 3 km. The 218 German rows were already 0.3 and keep their audit note. Roadmap point 215.

### JSON-in-cell field definitions (2 November 2026)

Scott approved on 2 November 2026. A survey of every table under `data/` found one column holding JSON in a cell, `strength_json` (31 claims). It is documented in `docs/json_fields.md` with a JSON Schema in `schema/json/strength_json.schema.json` (registry `schema/json/fields.json`). New checker `scripts/check_json_fields.py` validates every cell against its schema (also the copy in `build/all_claims.csv`) and checks that the schema enumerations equal `schema/vocabularies.json`; it runs in CI and in the local pre-push checks. New typed export `build/all_claims.json` (nested objects for JSON cells); no CSV column or value changed. Malformed cells found: none (62 cells checked, 0 problems), so no source row was changed.

### Unit lineage table (2 November 2026)

Scott approved on 2 November 2026 ("Let's create the linkage table and update our documentation"). New hand-maintained table `data/unit_lineage.csv` (schema `schema/unit_lineage.schema.json`, vocabulary `lineage_kind`: `redesignation`, `reconstitution_from_remnants`, `absorbed_into`, `formed_from_remnants`), checked by `scripts/validate.py` through `scripts/lineage.py` (units exist, kind and date basis valid, real dates within 1939-1945 and not before the predecessor's formed_date, references exist, no duplicate pair, no cycle, both units' unit_note names the other). New build outputs `build/units.csv` and `build/units.json` (data/units.csv plus `predecessor_unit_ids` and `successor_unit_ids`); no existing column changed. Semantic scenario 94. Roadmap point 216.

Rows `UL001` to `UL009`, each checked against the Lexikon der Wehrmacht pages (which follow Tessin) or the cited sources:
- **Reconstitution from remnants:** 47th (`UL001`, 17 Sep 1944), 272nd (`UL002`, 17 Sep 1944), 277th (`UL003`, 4 Sep 1944), 352nd (`UL005`, 21 Sep 1944; a US archival unit history gives 14 Sep), 363rd (`UL006`, 17 Sep 1944) Infantry Division to Volksgrenadier Division.
- **Redesignation:** 347th ID to VGD (`UL004`, 7 May 1945), 462nd ID to VGD (`UL007`, November 1944), Panzer Group West to Fifth Panzer Army (`UL009`, 5 August 1944; 1 August in Wikipedia, conflict group G020).
- **Formed from remnants:** 25th Panzergrenadier Division to Panzer Brigade 107 (`UL008`, 28 July 1944; 1 August in Lexikon). The brigade's return to the division in November 1944 is not a row (it would be a cycle).
- **Not linked (a new division reusing the number, no remnants named):** 276th (from the 580th VGD), 326th (from the 579th VGD), 361st (from the 569th VGD after the 361st ID was dissolved on 5 August 1944; the 361st ID was itself re-formed from the destroyed VGD in March 1945), 708th (the 573rd VGD renamed; Lexikon: the ID "was replaced"). The Reserve Corps renamings (LXII, LXIV, LXVI) are not linked because the Reserve Corps have no unit record.

`data/units.csv` `unit_note`, 18 units: GER-INF-047D, GER-VG-047D, GER-INF-272D, GER-VG-272D, GER-INF-277D, GER-VG-277D, GER-INF-347D, GER-VG-347D, GER-INF-352D, GER-VG-352D, GER-INF-363D, GER-VG-363D, GER-INF-462D, GER-VG-462D, GER-PG-025D: old value blank, new value a "Lineage: ..." sentence naming the linked record and row; GER-PZB-107B, GER-PZG-WESTA, GER-PZA-005A: old value the existing note, new value the same note with a "Lineage: ..." sentence appended. Reason: Scott approved the lineage table on 2 November 2026; the methodology asks for a unit_note on each linked record.

Scott approved on 2 November 2026 (outside-review points 4 and 5). New `scripts/coverage.py`, run by `scripts/build.py`, writes `build/coverage_by_unit.csv` (319 units), `build/coverage_by_month.csv` (96 nationality-months) and `build/review_queue.csv` (319 units ranked; weights gap 0.30, map-only 0.25, low confidence 0.15, single source family 0.15, empty months 0.15). ## nara-microfilm: NARA T-315 roll 440 (7th Parachute Division miscellaneous file; 3 November 2026)

Roll 440 (7. Fallschirmjäger-Division Ia item 77125, 576 pages) read in the German original on 2-3 November 2026, all pages by OCR. Almost all of it is administration of the Fallschirm-Jäger-Lehr-Regiment, April-August 1944 (not a dataset unit). The one division document, 13 February 1945 (page 8), cites the LXXXVI Corps and agrees with existing row `C1131` but gives no place. **No claims, places or references added; no existing row changed.** Reading notes private.

## nara-microfilm: NARA T-314 roll 1607, second pass on pages 743-871 (3 November 2026)

Pages 743-871 of roll 1607 (`R798`), left over from the first pass, rendered and read in the German original on 3 November 2026 (pages 836 and 843 unreadable). They hold LXXXVI Corps Anlagen of 3-14 June 1944 and section activity reports for January-June 1944. They confirm `C7135` (Dax, 15 June 1944) and agree with `C2076` (the Orne sector, late June). The move of the corps command post to Herm (16 February 1944) falls before the dataset period. **No claims, places or references added; no existing row changed.** Reading notes private.

## nara-microfilm: NARA T-354 roll 130 (2nd SS Panzer Division files 1940-45; 3 November 2026)

Roll 130 (NAID 267487470, 1278 frames) read in the German original on 3 November 2026. Frames 1-875, which hold the 1943-45 material, were read by OCR. The remaining frames (1940-41, and the 3rd SS Panzer Division 1938-41) fall before the dataset period. The 1944-45 papers are personnel and replacement paperwork with no places, plus a war game of 6 October 1944 set in Luxembourg (frames 56-59). The war game describes an exercise, not a real position, and is not used. **No claims, places or references added; no existing row changed.** Reading notes private.

## nara-microfilm: NARA T-315 roll 1725 (246th Infantry Division, Eastern Front 1943-44; 3 November 2026)

Roll 1725 (1441 pages) checked in the German original on 3 November 2026: the target cards and 13 sample pages spread across the roll. The whole roll covers the Eastern Front (VI and LIII Corps, Vitebsk-Dukhovshchina area) from July 1943 to January 1944, outside the dataset's theatre and period. It was not read page by page. **No claims, places or references added; no existing row changed.** Reading notes private.

## nara-microfilm: NARA T-315 roll 303 (5th Mountain Division 1943 / 5th Parachute Division 1944; 3 November 2026)

Roll 303 (603 pages) read in the German original on 3 November 2026. The 5th Mountain Division part (1943, outside the period) was sampled. The 5th Parachute Division file (27 March-5 December 1944, pages 220-603) was read in full by OCR. It holds discipline, court, personnel and gas-defence papers. Its command-post headers give no place and mention no dated unit location. **No claims, places or references added; no existing row changed.** Reading notes private.

## fms-fold3-netherlands: FMS B-093, Meindl, II Parachute Corps in the Rhineland (3 November 2026)

FMS B-093 (Eugen Meindl, written at Allendorf, 31 March 1947; German original with the English translation by H. Heitman and Hoehne's 1950 index; NARA M1035 fiche 0089G, 75 images) was read in full in the Fold3 viewer on 3 November 2026 and added as reference `R806` (source family `fms_german_manuscript`). Of 12 checkable facts, 8 agree with the dataset; the differences are the fall of Xanten (7 March, against Stacey's 8 March; roadmap 250), the Wesel bridgehead command (6 March, against `C2234`'s 7 March; roadmap 251), the 8th Parachute Division forming at Roermond (against `C1132`, a Kleve listing row) and the II Parachute Corps staff at St. Hubert near Kempen in December (against `C1071`, a Tilburg listing row).

- **New claims:** `C7689`-`C7721` (33, all German, blank parents, 0.5 for dated command posts in the text, 0.3 for sectors, approximate dates and sketch positions). They cover the II Parachute Corps command posts from Wahn and Kleve (September 1944), the Reichswald bunker (November), St. Hubert near Kempen (December), Sonsbeck (19 February 1945), Hoerstgen (3 March), Westermann's farm north of Alpsray (4 March), Perrich (6 March, uncertain reading of "Ferrich"), east of Bislich (10 March) and south-east of Bocholt (12 March), plus division sectors (84th, 190th, 180th Infantry; 6th, 7th, 8th Parachute; 116th Panzer) and the XLVII Panzer Corps moves.
- **New places:** `PL3539` Alpsray (Rheinberg), `PL3540` Perrich (Wesel), `PL3541` Ossenberg (Rheinberg), `PL3542` Bislich (Wesel), `PL3543` Bocholt (OpenStreetMap Nominatim, `R002`).

## fms-fold3-netherlands: FMS B-717, Student, First Parachute Army and Army Group H (3 November 2026)

FMS B-717 (Kurt Student, written in hospital at Rotenburg/Hannover, 20 November 1947, as a supplement to Geyer's MS B-147; English translation and German original; NARA M1035 fiche 0690, 47 images) was read in full in the Fold3 viewer on 3 November 2026 and added as reference `R807` (`fms_german_manuscript`). - the Army Group H handover on 31 January against 28-29 January (`C1128`, `C7443`; roadmap 252).

  - The XLVII Panzer Corps and 15th Panzergrenadier attack at Meijel, with the US 7th Armored (identification uncertain).
  - Army Group H at Hilversum (end of October), and the 2nd, 3rd and 5th Parachute Divisions re-raising.

  - `C0825`, `C0827`, `C1767`, `C1768`: unchanged, already 0.7 or more.
  - `C0856`: unchanged, same family as B-093.
  - `C0826`, `C0908`, `C1037`, `C2161`, `C7131`, `C7133`, `C7438`: unchanged. Agreement is undated or approximate.

## fms-fold3-netherlands: FMS B-084, Schlemm, First Parachute Army, re-read in full (3 November 2026)

FMS B-084 (`R777`, Alfred Schlemm, Island Farm, 21 April 1946; NARA M1035, 76 images, German with the English translation and Appendices 1-4) was re-read in full in the Fold3 viewer on 3 November 2026. The first reading had taken only `C2226`-`C2235`.

`data/references_meta.csv`, row R777:
- **reliability_note:** old value the note from the first reading (31 October 2026), new value the same note plus a sentence on the full re-read and the LXV/LXIII naming.
- **used_for:** old value covered the Rhineland battle, new value covers 20 November 1944 to 10 March 1945.
- **Reason:** the scope of the reading was widened.

Of 13 checkable facts, 10 agree with the dataset. The differences:
- The fall of Xanten: "2-3 March" against 8 March (roadmap 250 updated).
- The Wesel bridges blown at 07:00 on 10 March, against Meindl's about 05:00. Reading notes only.
- Abraham's corps: "LXV Corps" in the text, shown on Skizze 2 near Kaldenkirchen while the redraw puts it at Moers. The rows use GER-COR-063C with a note.

- **New claims:** `C7748`-`C7777` (30, all German, blank parents). They cover:
  - Schlemm taking command on 20 November.
  - The end-January positions: the army at Dinxperlo, II Parachute Corps at St. Hubert, LXXXVI Corps at Appeldorn, and the 8th Parachute Division's Roer bridgehead.
  - Abraham's corps: subordinated about 18 February, near Kaldenkirchen on 25 February, its southern front and command post at Moers on 27 February, the Issum-Homberg sector on 4 March, Repelen on 5 March, and its staff to the east bank on 6 March.
  - The 15th Panzergrenadier and Panzer Lehr Divisions put under it.
  - LXXXVI Corps at Geldern on 25 February and 1 March, and its staff withdrawn on 3 March.
  - The XLVII Panzer Corps staff to the east bank on 6 March. B-093 is cited as well, but it is the same family, so the row stays at 0.5.
  - Four division rows for 24 February and nine for 4 March, all at 0.3 except the 15th Panzergrenadier at Marienbaum (0.5).
- **New places:** `PL3549` Appeldorn, `PL3550` Sint Odiliënberg, `PL3551` Kaldenkirchen, `PL3552` Moers, `PL3553` Kamp-Lintfort, `PL3554` Repelen (OpenStreetMap Nominatim, `R002`).
- **Corroborations:** R777 appended on 9 rows, with old and new values in each row's notes. All confidences are unchanged, for these reasons:
  - `C7510`: conflict group G056.
  - `C7707`, `C7710`: same family.
  - `C7509`, `C7511`, `C7512`, `C7514`, `C7515`: Schlemm's 24 February against the Stacey 25 February rows.
  - `C7531`: approximate date.
- **Semantic tests:** scenario 133.
- Reading notes (private, Drive only): `FMS_B-084_notes.md`.

## fms-fold3-netherlands: FMS B-368, Langhaeuser, 6th Parachute Division (3 November 2026)

FMS B-368 (Rudolf Langhaeuser, Allendorf, 6 February 1947; attested by Plocher, reviewed by Geyer; NARA M1035 fiche 0363G, 57 images, German with the English translation and Anlagen 1-4; the narrative ends on 10 March 1945) was read in full in the Fold3 viewer on 3 November 2026 and added as reference `R808` (`fms_german_manuscript`). Of 12 checkable facts, 8 agree with the dataset. The differences, all dates:
- the Kapelsche Veer given up in mid-January, against 30-31 January (G059);
- the relief of the 84th Division staff about 10 February, against 16 February (G060);
- the 711th Division leaving about 11 December, against 26 December (roadmap 256);
- the 80 km front by about 20 December while Stacey has the 712th Division at the Kapelsche Veer until 31 December.

- **New claims:** `C7778`-`C7794` (17: 16 German, 1 Allied; blank parents). They cover:
  - The re-forming at Assen-Meppel-Coevorden (October 1944).
  - The Arnhem sector from 19 November, with the command post at Velp; the 363rd Volksgrenadier and 10th SS Panzer Divisions relieved; the 50th Division opposite.
  - The 2nd Parachute Division's relief of the division at Arnhem (about 10 December).
  - The Waal and Maas sector, with command posts at Gameren (11 December), Brakel (7 January) and Geldermalsen (17 January), and the 711th Division relieved near Heesselt.
  - The 80 km front (about 20 December) and the Kapelsche Veer given up.
  - Kampfgruppe Plocher (about 10 February), the regiments arriving at Marienbaum, and Grieth and Wissel.
  - The east-bank sector Haffen-Hamminkeln (10 March).
- **New places:** `PL3555` Gameren, `PL3556` Heesselt, `PL3557` Brakel, `PL3558` Grieth (OpenStreetMap Nominatim, `R002`).

  - `C1057`, `C1545`, `C7439`, `C7459`, `C7484`, `C7485`, `C7488`: unchanged. Agreement is undated or approximate. `C1545` and `C7459` are candidates for a raise if Scott accepts undated agreement.
- **Conflict groups:**
  - **G059 (roadmap 254):** `C7442` preferred. `C7790`: conflict_group_id old value blank, new value G059; claim_status old value preferred, new value alternate; confidence unchanged (0.3).
  - **G060 (roadmap 255):** `C2027` preferred. `C7791`: conflict_group_id old value blank, new value G060; claim_status old value preferred, new value alternate; confidence unchanged (0.3).

## fms-fold3-netherlands: FMS B-156, Reinhard, LXXXVIII Corps from the Albert Canal to the lower Maas (3 November 2026)

FMS B-156 (Hans W. Reinhard, commanding general of LXXXVIII Corps, Allendorf, 8 June 1946; subject index by Gustav Hoehne, about 1950; NARA M1035 fiche 0150G, 43 images, German only) was read in full in the Fold3 viewer on 3 November 2026 and added as reference `R811` (`fms_german_manuscript`). It covers the corps from June 1944 (the Dutch sea front) and in detail from 4 September to 21 December 1944. It agrees with the dataset on the command post sequence (Bilthoven, Moergestel, Dongen, Genderen, Almkerk, Noordeloos, Geldermalsen, Houten, Bilthoven) and on the handover to Schwalbe on 24 December (roadmap 258 updated). The differences, all dates: Herpt at the end of September against 27 October (roadmap 260); the attack on 's-Hertogenbosch in the first third of October against 22-28 October (roadmap 260, no new row); Chill to Bergen op Zoom on 20/21 October against 7-8 October (roadmap 261); the 6th Parachute Division under the corps at the end of November against 19 December (roadmap 262); the 245th Division at Bladel about 16 September against South Beveland on 17 September (roadmap 263).

- **Source family:** B-156 is one family with B-475, B-717, B-774 and B-762 (all FMS manuscripts written in Allied custody in 1946-47, partly by officers of the same staff), so it raises no row supported only by FMS manuscripts.
- **New claims:** `C7849`-`C7897` (49: 45 German, 4 Allied from the German account; blank parents): Reinhard on the Dutch sea front (June); the 347th Division leaving for the Charleville-Namur area; the Bilthoven order of 4 September; the 719th Division over the Moerdijk bridges, at Beringen, at Merksem (with the British armoured attack there, identification uncertain) and the loss of Beringen; Chill's decision at Turnhout (2 September), his counter-attack from Tessenderlo, Geel and the Aart crossing (with the 15th Scottish Division); Division Erdmann at Hechtel; the withdrawal to the Herentals canal line; the 245th Division at Bladel, Reusel and Best; the corps at Moergestel on 17 September; the 101st Airborne at Best; the 59th Division at Son, Veghel, Koevering and west of the Best-Sint-Oedenrode road; the command posts Dongen (19-20 September), Herpt (end of September, as dated), Genderen (end of October); the corps under the Fifteenth Army (about 20 September); Chill at Goirle, Dinther and his move of 20/21 October; the 712th Division at Nuland, Zaltbommel, Tiel and Ochten; the Hedel bridgehead (held from early October, given up at the end of November); the lines of end October and early November; the Maas crossing; the 256th Volksgrenadier Division command post at Almkerk; the three-division line at the end of November (711th, 712th, 6th Parachute).
- **Inferred values:** `C7858` (719th Division, loss of Beringen): date old value none, new value 1944-09-07, reason: the text dates the event 6 September forenoon and the unit already has a row on 6 September (Merksem).  `C7866` (245th Division at Reusel-Middelbeers): date old value 1944-09-17 (first entry on this branch), new value 1944-09-20, reason: the text gives no day ("soon" after about 16 September). `C7876` (59th Division at Koevering): date old value none, new value 1944-09-24, reason: the text gives no day; placed in its sequence.
- **New places:** `PL3573` Tessenderlo, `PL3574` Geel, `PL3575` Herentals, `PL3576` Bladel, `PL3577` Reusel (coordinates old value 51.4571, 5.1770, the Nominatim hit, new value 51.3620, 5.1650, the village centre, reason: the text means the village), `PL3578` Koevering, `PL3579` Sint-Oedenrode, `PL3580` Loon op Zand, `PL3581` Capelle, `PL3582` Zaltbommel, `PL3583` Ochten (OpenStreetMap Nominatim, `R002`).
- **Uncertain readings (kept in the notes of the rows):** Infantry Regiment "743" at Merksem; "Tenderloo" (Tessenderlo) "west of Baden"; the crossing "Aart"; the panzer formation "102"; "Gorindon" (Gorinchem); "Hoesselt" (probably Heesselt); "Gent" (probably Gendt); "Honten" (Houten); "Halderen" (Haalderen); the date "4." or "A." September on p.36.
- **Corroborations:** B-156 appended on 51 rows (reference_ids, source and source_locator; old and new values in each row's notes).
  - `C0220` (101st Airborne Division lands between Son and Veghel, 17 September): confidence old value 0.5, new value 0.7. Reason: corroboration rule; the CMH order of battle and B-156 agree on unit, date and place.
  - 50 rows unchanged: 34 are FMS-only rows (same family); 16 agree only approximately in date or place, are map rows (`C3028`, `C3200`), are already 0.7 (`C1458`, `C1462`) or are named in a roadmap point (`C7435`).
- **Roadmap:** 258 updated (third source dates the handover 24 December; grouping recommended); 260-263 opened.
- **Tests:** scenario 138 added.

## fms-fold3-german-2: FMS B-262, Blauensteiner, II Parachute Corps between Maas and Rhine (3 November 2026)

FMS B-262 (Oberst i.G. Ernst Blauensteiner, chief of staff of the II Parachute Corps, Allendorf, 20 December 1946; subject index by Alfred Zerbel, 16 November 1950; NARA M1035 fiches 0253 and 0257G, 42 images on Fold3) was read in full on 3 November 2026 and added as reference `R813` (`fms_german_manuscript`). Differences in the translation, all corrected in the rows: Wehrkreiskommando VII for VI (Korps Feldt); "beginning of December" for mid-December (command post Vorst to St. Hubert); two parachute regiments for one (Fallschirmjäger-Regiment 2 under the 190th Division); "Hanten" for Xanten.

- **New claims** `C7907`-`C7940` (34, all German; blank parents; strengths in the notes only), places `PL3589` Bergen (Limburg), `PL3590` Vorst, `PL3591` Elmpter Wald, `PL3592` Linne. Corps move to the Wasserburg at Kleve (18 September, 0.5), Korps Feldt north of the Reichswald, the defence line Cuijk-Groesbeek-Erlekom, the 190th and 84th Division staffs, Kampfgruppe Katzmann and the extension to Bergen, the failed mid-October night attack at Groesbeek, the move to Roermond-Venlo, the Venlo bridgehead (180th), the 7th and 8th Parachute Divisions, the Vorst command post, the 7th Parachute Division to Strasbourg, the planned Well-Velden attack, the Linne salient, Division 406, LXIII Corps taking the old sector, the Goch sector, Repelen, Alpen and the Wesel bridgehead (15th Panzergrenadier, 116th Panzer and Panzer Lehr remnants).
- **Sketch (map) rows at 0.3:** `C7913` (190th, Sketch 1), `C7914` (corps headquarters near Keppeln, Sketch 1, reading uncertain), `C7923`, `C7924` (7th and 8th Parachute Divisions, Sketch 2), `C7934` (15th Panzergrenadier near Udem, Sketch 3). Other sketch labels (corps boundaries, the 84th Division on Sketch 1, HQ symbols near Birten/Veen on Sketch 3) duplicate text rows, fall on a day with another row for the unit, or are illegible; they are in the notes only.
- **Inferred dates:** `C7909` 22 September (old value none, new value 1944-09-22; reason: the change to defence after 19 September has no day, and the corps has rows on 19-21 September); `C7927` 25 January 1945 (old value none, new value 1945-01-25; reason: "end of January", before the corps row of 31 January); `C7925` 31 December 1944 (old value none, new value 1944-12-31; reason: "end of December", kept after the 12th Army Group map row of 26 December to avoid an impossible move speed). Each row says so.
- **Placeholder promoted:** `GER-DIV-406` (record_status placeholder to active) for `C7931`.
- **Corroborations (24):** `R813` appended to `C7689`, `C7690`, `C7691`, `C7692`, `C0856`, `C7113`, `C7695`, `C7696`, `C7700`, `C7698`, `C7702`, `C7699`, `C2235`, `C7499`, `C2232`, `C7543`, `C2234`, `C7715`, `C2229`, `C7713`, `C7716` (confidence unchanged: same source family as B-093/B-084, or approximate), and `C7532` and `C7533` (Stacey, 28 February 1945, Kevelaer: the 190th Division passes to the 2nd Parachute Corps): confidence old value 0.3, new value 0.7; reason: corroboration rule, a second source family (`fms_german_manuscript`) agrees on unit, date and named place.

### FMS B-414 (Geyer, Army Group H / OB Nordwest, 10 March-9 May 1945; branch `fms-fold3-german-2`, 3 November 2026)
- **Source:** `R814`, Oberst i.G. Rolf Geyer, *Darstellung des Verlaufs der Kämpfe auf deutscher Seite bei Heeresgruppe H vom 10.3. bis 9.5.1945*, MS B-414 (written 20 April 1946 at Bruges/Allendorf; German index by Koch-Erpach, 3 February 1951), read on Fold3 (141 images). The German original (images 54-141, with Anlagen 1-6 and Skizzen 1-6) is primary; the English translation (images 1-53) was used only to check. Translation errors recorded: the front of 30/31 March ran along the IJssel to Brummen, not the Lippe; LXVII Corps, not LXIII; Bislich, not Mislich; Schlemm "ausgefallen" (put out of action), not killed; XXXXVII (XLVII) Panzer Corps, not XXXVII; Ahaus, not Almaus; Goor, not Goch.
- **New claims (43, all German, blank parents):** `C7941`-`C7983`. Anlage 6 command posts as text rows (`C7941`-`C7948`: Zutphen for Voorden-Zutphen, Oldenzaal, north-west of Bersenbrück, Delmenhorst at 0.3 for an uncertain reading, north of Soltau, Reinbek-Wohltorf, Langwedel (Skizze 6 has nearby Nortorf from 23 April; kept in the note), Kollerup); the March grouping (LXVII Corps at Dinslaken, 245th at Coesfeld, 190th to Amersfoort, Division 406 staff to the 149th Field Training Division at Zwolle, Twenty-fifth Army at Hilversum); the Rhine crossing (Kleffel and Blumentritt from 25 March, XLVII Panzer Corps to Dorsten-Kirchhellen, 190th to Kirchhellen, 116th Panzer at Hünxe on 25 March, LXVII Corps back to the Autobahn, 180th and LXVII under XLVII Panzer Corps on 26 March); 28 March-6 April (245th to Cloppenburg, LXXXVI Corps broken north of the Lippe side canal and at Münster, First Parachute Army command posts Havixbeck and Iburg, Division 471 on Rheine-Lengerich-Iburg, the IJssel-Lochem-Rheine front, Armeegruppe Student north of Lingen, II Parachute Corps toward Lingen, the 6 April Küstenkanal-Cloppenburg front, Student/Blumentritt staff at Celle); Skizze 5 positions (7th and 8th Parachute Divisions at Friesoythe, 471 and 15th Panzergrenadier at Wildeshausen); April-May (XXXIX Panzer Corps from Uelzen to Gifhorn, 84th remnants, II Parachute Corps to Cuxhaven-Bremerhaven, Blumentritt at Bad Bramstedt, 245th at Lauenburg 28 April-1 May, 8th Parachute Division toward Ratzeburg [?], Armeeabteilung Straube).
- **Places:** `PL3593`-`PL3610` (Oldenzaal, Bersenbrück, Soltau, Wohltorf, Langwedel, Kollerup, Coesfeld, Dorsten, Kirchhellen, Hünxe, Cloppenburg, Havixbeck, Bad Iburg, Lengerich, Gifhorn, Cuxhaven, Bad Bramstedt, Ratzeburg).
- **Placeholder promoted:** `GER-OBNW-000H` (OB Nordwest) for `C7945`-`C7948`.
- **Corroborations (23):** `R814` appended to `C1201`, `C1202`, `C7566`, `C1181`, `C7567`, `C7565`, `C1182`, `C2025`, `C1185`, `C7568`, `C1238`, `C1237`, `C1223`, `C7619`, `C1263`, `C1245`, `C1532`, `C7834`, `C7835`, `C7683`, `C7681` (confidence unchanged: same FMS family, approximate, representative point, map-only or already 0.7), and raised: `C2026` (2nd Parachute Division at Dinslaken, 24 March) and `C1183` (15th Panzergrenadier counterattack at Rees, 24 March): confidence old value 0.3 and 0.5, new value 0.7; reason: corroboration rule, a second source family (`fms_german_manuscript`) agrees with `us_army_cmh` on unit, date and named place.
- **Coordinates:** no existing coordinates were changed. Four new speed warnings are explained in `data/coordinate_exceptions.csv` (`C6166`/`C7950`, `C7943`/`C7967`, `C1223`/`C7971`, `C7603`/`C7979`). Before commit, seven new `current_activity` texts were shortened (full text in the definition), the 22-23 March rows moved to `OP-CAMP-CENTRAL-EUROPE` (the Rhineland campaign ends 21 March), and `C7977` (corps reaches Gifhorn) set to 10 km precision as a corps-level point.

### FMS B-147 (Geyer, Army Group H in the Rhineland, 10 November 1944-10 March 1945; branch `fms-fold3-german-2`, 3 November 2026)
- **Source:** `R815`, Oberst i.G. Rolf Geyer (Ia Army Group H), *Operation "Rheinland" ... Darstellung des Verlaufs der Kämpfe auf der deutschen Seite vom 10.11.1944-10.3.1945 (Heeresgruppe "H")*, MS B-147 (1946; German original only; index by Gustav Höhne, 21 September 1950), read on Fold3 (combined B-147/148, 107 images; B-147 images 1-89).
- **New claims (19, all German, blank parents):** `C7984`-`C8002`: XXX Corps about Rotterdam on Skizze 1 (map, 0.3); the Twenty-fifth Army taking over the AOK 15 sector about 25 November (Christiansen) and given the Rhine bank to Emmerich about 2 March; the First Parachute Army back to the Maas by 25 November, out of the Roermond bend (Venlo-Kaldenkirchen-Waldniel), on Uerdingen-Krefeld-Kempen-Wachtendonck, on Rheinhausen-Aldekerk-Geldern, the Xanten-Sonsbeck-Rheinberg front and the 6 March bridgehead line; XLVII Panzer Corps to Xanten under the army on 9 February; the 34th SS Division into the Maas-Waal front; Panzer Lehr at Sonsbeck (23 February) and taken to Rheindahlen (24 February); Division 406 at Dülken-Viersen and on the Elmpter Forst-Waldniel security line; the 2nd Parachute Division turned south of Krefeld; the 15th Panzergrenadier Division to Sonsbeck (26 February) and its parts north of Isselburg (early March); the 245th arriving about Borken.
- **Places:** `PL3611`-`PL3616` (Rheindahlen, Waldniel, Kempen, Aldekerk, Borken, Isselburg).
- **Corroborations (24):** `R815` appended to `C1037`, `C1039`, `C7443`, `C1128` (Anlage 1 dates Blaskowitz to 1 February), `C7449`, `C7450`, `C7457`, `C7465`, `C7491`, `C7930`, `C7931`, `C7932`, `C1153`, `C7531`, `C7756`, `C7760`, `C7764`, `C7763`, `C7710`, `C7711`, `C7548`, `C7712`, `C7936`, `C2229`; no raises (same FMS family as B-093/B-262/B-414, approximate, or already 0.7).

- **Tests:** scenario 142 added. Next free: `C8003`, `PL3617`, `R816`, `G063`, scenario 143, roadmap 267.

## fms-fold3-german-2: pre-invasion dates allowed (4 November 2026)

- **Decision (Scott, 4 November 2026):** pre-invasion data may be entered with real dates; the dataset may eventually cover the whole war.
- **Validator:** `scripts/operations.py` old rule "every claim needs a CMH campaign streamer covering its date" (an effective 6 June 1944 floor), new rule: a streamer is required only from the first streamer date (6 June 1944); dates before 1 September 1939 are an error (sanity bound). README, HANDOFF and schema README updated.
- **New operation:** `OP-PRE-INVASION` (umbrella, 1 September 1939-5 June 1944, derived).
- **Re-dated rows (forced 6 June notes):**
  - `C8212` (II Parachute Corps, Quintin): date old value 1944-06-06, new value 1944-05-05; operation_id old value OP-CAMP-NORMANDY, new value OP-PRE-INVASION; confidence 0.3 unchanged. Reason: B-240 has the move in early May; day inferred.
  - `C8213` (3rd Parachute Division, Huelgoat) and `C8214` (5th Parachute Division, Rennes): date old value 1944-06-06, new value 1944-05-15; operation_id as above; confidence 0.3 unchanged. Reason: B-240 gives early or mid-May; day inferred.
  - `C8234` (LXVII Corps, Amiens): date old value 1944-06-06, new value 1944-06-05; operation_id as above; confidence 0.3 unchanged. Reason: B-236 has the corps in position before 7 June, B-596 at Amiens from about January 1944; day inferred as the eve of the invasion.
- **Not changed:** `C2060`-`C2068` (R761 armoured dawn positions on 6 June) are D-Day positions, not forced dates. **Tests:** scenario 168; scenario 164 expectation updated.


## fms-fold3-german-2: run_checks exits non-zero on any failing step (4 November 2026)

- **Fix:** the local verification script, old value `UnitLocationData-tools/run_checks.sh` (outside the repo; piped every step through `grep`/`tail`, so it exited 0 even when `validate.py` reported errors), new value `scripts/run_checks.sh` in the repo (`set -u -o pipefail`; each step's own exit status is recorded and any failure ends with `CHECKS FAILED: ...` and exit 1). Steps: validate, check_json_fields, semantic_tests, build --check, build_references --check, review_sample --check, check_coordinates, audit_command_location, audit_temporal_relationships, check_claim_ids (when origin/main is available). The tools copy is now a wrapper that calls it.
- **Proof:** a temporary row dated 1938-05-15 (`C8214`) made validate, semantic_tests and build --check fail and the script exit 1; after reverting the row it prints `ALL CHECKS PASSED` and exits 0. No data changed.

## fms-fold3-german-2: FMS B-023 German original with English check, Heilmann, 5th Parachute Division in the Ardennes 1 December 1944-12 January 1945 (4 November 2026)

- **Source:** FMS B-023 (`R843`, new), German original (Fold3 images 62-117) read in full as primary, English translation (images 1-61) checked against it; undated, German index sheet 20 July 1950 [?]. The English garbles several facts (15 for 16 December, Martelange for Harlange, Regiment 15 for 13, 50 casualties for 50 dead); the German is followed.
- **New claims (parents blank; 0.5 unless stated):** 5th Parachute Division `C8286` Bitburg-Oberweis-Sülm area end of November (0.3; old value none, new value 30 November, reason: only "end of November"), `C8287` map exercise at Sülm 13 December (0.3), `C8288` Regiment 14 at Putscheid 16 December, `C8289` Regiment 15 at the Bourscheid crossing 17 December, `C8290` Hoscheid 18 December, `C8291` command post Weiler 19 December, `C8292` Nothum crossroads and `C8293` Wiltz taken 20 December (claim-key exception), `C8294` command post Berlé 21 December, `C8295` Martelange reported taken 21 December, `C8296` forward command post Losange 23 December, `C8297` front Lutrebois-Harlange-Bavigne 27 December, `C8298` command post north of Doncols 28 December, `C8299` Harlange, Villers-la-Bonne-Eau and Lutremange given up 7 January, `C8300` squeezed round Doncols and `C8301` artillery at Derenbach 8 January; LXXXV Corps `C8302` command post near Dodenburg early December (0.3; old value none, new value 5 December, reason: only "early December"); Panzer Lehr Division `C8303` Consthum 17 December, `C8304` Lutremange 22 December, `C8305` staff at Losange 23 December; 26th Volksgrenadier Division `C8306` commander at Hompré 22 December (0.3); 1st SS Panzer Division `C8307` Lutremange-Villers-la-Bonne-Eau about 29 December (0.3; old value none, new value 29 December, reason: placed only between 28 December and the attack of `C7154`), `C8308` withdrawn 6 January; US 28th Infantry Division `C8309` opposite Vianden 15 December (0.3, German intelligence).
- **New places:** `PL3749`-`PL3763` (Sülm, Putscheid, Hoscheid, Weiler, Nothum, Berlé, Losange, Villers-la-Bonne-Eau, Doncols, Harlange, Derenbach, Dodenburg, Consthum, Lutremange, Hompré; Nominatim).
- **Corroborations:** `R843` appended to `C0997`, `C0982`, `C7178`, `C7208`, `C7154`, `C0557`, `C4899` (confidence unchanged: no exact day or place in the manuscript, map row, or already at the rubric level) and `C7137` (26th Volksgrenadier Division closing the Bastogne ring, 22 December): confidence old value 0.5, new value 0.7, reason: an independent family (FMS) agrees with the captured German records (`R799`) on the day and the approximate place.
- **Strengths in notes only** (fighting value IV on 1 December; about 2,000 men of the fighting reserve at Trier; 30 assault guns, about half ready; 50 dead from the Westwall to Bastogne). **Open:** roadmap 327-328. **Tests:** scenario 173. Next free: `C8310`, `PL3764`, `R844`, `G065`, scenario 174, roadmap 329.

## fms-fold3-german-2: small units traced to a division; B-023 regiments, engineer battalion and assault-gun brigade (4 November 2026)

- **Rule (Scott, 4 November 2026):** units down to company level are recorded only when a source traces them to a parent division or higher; untraceable small units are left out; divisions and above stay the focus. Documented in HANDOFF section 3 (rule 17) and `schema/README.md`. No validator change was needed: `regiment/battle_group` and `battalion` were already allowed. The claim `parent_unit_id` stays blank (standing rule); the link is the units.csv organic parent plus a relation.
- **New units:** `GER-FJR-013`, `GER-FJR-014`, `GER-FJR-015` (regiments), `GER-FJPI-005` (Fallschirm-Pionier-Bataillon 5), all with units.csv `parent_unit_id` old value blank (new rows), new value `GER-FJ-005D`, reason: B-023 order of battle (German p.1-2); `GER-STUG-011B` Sturmgeschütz-Brigade 11 (brigade, Army; no organic parent, reason: an army assault-gun brigade attached for the offensive).
- **Relations:** `CR1067`-`CR1070` organic and `CR1071` attached to `GER-FJ-005D`; start = first claim (basis claim_date, inferred), open at the last claim; links observed_during.
- **Claims (R843, blank parents):** `C8310`-`C8313` Regiment 14 (Putscheid, Nothum, Lutrebois-Villers-la-Bonne-Eau, Doncols), `C8314`-`C8315` Regiment 15 (Bourscheid, Harlange), `C8316` Regiment 13 (Tintange-Boulaide-Surre), `C8317`-`C8319` engineers (Vianden, Wiltz, Sainlez about 26 December, 0.3; old value none, new value 26 December, reason: only "the following days" after 25 December), `C8320`-`C8321` assault guns (ford near Vianden, Martelange). The division rows stay. Scenario 174.

## fms-fold3-german-2: FMS B-361 German original, Blumentritt, Armee Blumentritt 10 April-31 May 1945 (4 November 2026)

- **Source:** FMS B-361 (`R844`, new), German original (Fold3 images 35-79) read in full; the English translation (images 1-34) was **not** checked. Allendorf, February 1947.
- **Headquarters sequence (`GER-ARMY-BLUMT`, sketch 4 and text):** `C8322` Celle 10 April (0.5), `C8323` camp east of Soltau about 12 April (0.3; old value none, new value 12 April, reason: "11 or 12 April"), `C8324` Nutzfelde [?] about 14 April (0.3; new value 14 April, reason: between "to about 13" and "to about 15 April"), `C8325` Gülzow [?] about 16 April to 23 April (0.3; new value 16 April, reason: follows No. 3), No. 5 Bad Bramstedt area from 24 April = existing `C7980` (R844 appended), `C8326` Hohenaspe from 3 May (0.5), `C8327` capitulation on the battlefield 5 May (0.5), `C8328` Wentorf near Bergedorf about 10 May (0.3, approximate day). The dissolution of the staff at Wentorf on 31 May is not a row: no campaign streamer covers the date (roadmap 330).

Subordination (2nd Marine Division, Korps Ems with the 15th Panzergrenadier Division, Stäbe Holste and Witthöft, XXXIX Panzer Corps with Panzer Division Clausewitz, II Parachute Corps with the 8th Parachute Division, the 245th Division as OB Nordwest reserve) is recorded in the claim notes and roadmap 330, not as relations (single source, parents blank).
- **New places:** `PL3764`-`PL3767` (Nutzfelde, Gülzow, Hohenaspe, Wentorf bei Hamburg; Nominatim).
- **Corroborations (no raises, FMS family or no exact day):** `R844` appended to `C7976`, `C7977`, `C1263`, `C7980`, `C7981`.
- **Open:** roadmap 329-330. **Tests:** scenario 175. Next free: `C8331`, `PL3768`, `R845`, `CR1072`, `G065`, scenario 176, roadmap 331.

## map-overlay-plan: map overlay tool (5 November 2026)

- Added `tools/map_overlay/overlay.py` and `tools/map_overlay/README.md`. Old value: plan document only (`docs/map_overlay_plan.md`), no tool. New value: a CLI MVP that selects preferred and alternate claims within ±1 day of a sheet date, projects them with `tools/loc12ag/georef.py` `Fit.inv`, draws a marker, label and precision circle on the scan, and writes `overlay_<sheet>_<date>.png` plus a review CSV to a scratch folder. Reason: Scott asked to leave `map-overlay-plan` open and build the tool on it. Overlay outputs are not committed. No claim data rows changed.
